# Alpha Agents: Build Log

One entry after every unit and every playtest, newest at the bottom, so this file reads as a chain of development. Append only; never edit an earlier entry, add a new one that corrects it. Format:

## [date], [unit ID or "Playtest"], [short name]
Status: done, partial, or blocked
Summary: two to four sentences on what was built or tested and anything left open.
Bugs: none, or the lesson IDs for bugs found and fixed.
Commit: the commit hash, if any.

## 2026-10-01, P0-U2, Local environment
Status: done
Summary: One command boots the local stack: `pnpm dev:up` starts Postgres 16.15 and Redis 7.4.11 through Docker Compose (127.0.0.1:5432 and 127.0.0.1:6380, named volumes, health checks) and an anvil fork of Monad mainnet, and `pnpm dev:status`, `dev:down` and `dev:reset` (which asks for "reset" before deleting volumes) manage it; `pnpm run doctor` checks every prerequisite without printing the RPC URL. Foundry is pinned at 1.8.3 (cae51ad) in `.foundry-version`, forge-std 1.16.2 installs with Soldeer (D-148), and the fork is pinned at Monad mainnet block 109670000 in `chains/monad/fork.json`; anvil reports chain ID 143, network monad and hardfork MonadTen there, and all three fork smoke tests pass (chain ID, pinned block, ERC-6551 registry code). The RPC is not archive-capable, so the pin goes stale as the chain advances: Foundry's cache in `~/.foundry/cache` keeps repeat runs working for state already fetched, and the README explains how to re-pin when doctor fails on "RPC serves pinned block". During the session `.env` first held a Monad testnet RPC (chain 10143); the owner replaced it with a mainnet RPC before the pin was chosen. Suggestions for later units: have `dev:status` also check that anvil reports network monad; mirror ForkConfigTest's minimum-block floor in `scripts/lib/config.js`; the P0-U2 prompt and BUILD_PLAN say `pnpm doctor`, which should read `pnpm run doctor` (L-2); Q-23 still assumes an archive-capable fork RPC.
Bugs: L-2, L-3.
Commit: 9c7f631, 31916f2, 65817d0, aaa4672, aa343cf, 9e2e64d, b0b91c2, 5df4326, plus the log commit.

## 2026-10-01, P0-U3, Config, secrets, and environment IDs
Status: done
Summary: Added `@alpha-agents/config` (packages/config, TypeScript run directly by Node type stripping, zod 4.6.5): three environments selected by APP_ENV (local, the anvil fork of chain 143 at a fixed loopback RPC, labeled fork; testnet, chain 10143; beta, chain 143, labeled mainnet-beta per D-145), a registry of 43 variables marked secret or public, and `loadConfig`, which validates only the selected environment's variables, treats empty and placeholder values as not set (Alpha Markets lesson 25), wraps secrets in a self-redacting `Secret`, and throws value-free ConfigErrors; the mainnet guard refuses a signing service in beta without `BETA_SIGNING_ENABLED=true`, rejects that flag elsewhere, and rejects a testnet RPC equal to the mainnet URL, and `assertChainId` checks the reported chain. `.env.example` is generated from the registry (`pnpm run env:example`) with every variable of the full build, its environments and first unit; doctor, dev:up and test:fork use the shared loader, fork pins below block 100,000,000 are rejected as in ForkConfigTest, and dev:status requires anvil network monad. `pnpm run secrets:scan` and a new CI job run gitleaks v8.30.1 (pinned by digest) over the full history: the first full-history scan of 23 commits found one finding, a placeholder LiteLLM key (`sk-master-spike`) in a Planv1 spike plan, verified as a false positive and ignored by fingerprint; the final scan of 29 commits is clean, and a staged fake token was caught. Results: 89 vitest tests (67 in the config package, including secret-leak tests verified to fail when Secret leaks), lint, format, typecheck, forge build, non-fork forge tests, doctor, dev:up, dev:status, test:fork and the secrets scan all pass. BUILD_PLAN's P0-U3 asks for templates for five environments (local, fork, testnet, mainnet-beta, mainnet); this unit follows the prompt's three, with local being the fork and public mainnet deferred to Phase 9; environment IDs on every domain record type are P0-U5. BUILD_PLAN had no "pnpm doctor" mention; the three in the P0-U2 prompt are fixed. Suggestions: P0-U5 stamps `Environment.label` on records; doctor could also check MONAD_TESTNET_RPC_URL reports 10143 once P1-U3 uses it; add a `mainnet` environment at public launch; KMS and provider variable names are provisional until P1-U5, Q-08, Q-24 and Q-26 settle them; pin CI actions to commit SHAs.

## 2026-10-01, P0-U5, Shared domain and policy packages
Status: done

## 2026-10-01, P0-U6, Web foundation and design system
Status: done
Summary: Added P0-U6 to the plan (D-150; BUILD_PLAN unit 5, before P0-U4, which now depends on it) and built `apps/web`: Next.js 16.3.8 App Router, React 19.3, Tailwind CSS 4.3.3 and shadcn/ui 4.21.1 on Radix, every dependency pinned, Geist Sans and Mono self-hosted through next/font, and `pnpm dev:web`. Design tokens live only in `globals.css` (graphite #121412 layers, off-white, lime #b6ff3b, muted red #e57068, brass #c9a35c, a seven-step type scale, a 0.25rem spacing unit, radii, borders, shadows) as a palette plus semantic dark-theme tokens under `[data-theme]`; Tailwind's defaults are reset so only tokens compile, and a test fails on any raw color, size or font in a component or page. The generated shadcn components were rewritten on the tokens (Button, Card, Input and Field, Select, Slider, Tabs, Dialog, Tooltip, Toast, Skeleton, Table, EmptyState, Badge), plus StatusPill and ReasonMessage rendered straight from packages/domain, AmountDisplay and AddressDisplay on new domain helpers `formatAmount` and `shortenAddress`, StatBar, RiskBadge, DemandCounter, BetaBanner and the app shell (navigation that collapses below the large breakpoint, chain indicator with the environment label, placeholder wallet and notifications buttons). `/design` shows every token and every component in every state, using `data-force` with `is-hover` and `is-focus` variants for hover and focus, and every canonical mode and reason code. Tests: 453 vitest tests (119 in the new web project under jsdom), and Playwright screenshots at 1440px and 380px with exact comparison, an axe scan with no serious or critical violations, and a mobile navigation test, all in the Playwright 1.63.0 image pinned by digest, locally (`pnpm test:web:e2e`) and in a new CI web job; a clean-checkout simulation of that job in the same image passed, and CI itself runs after the push. P0-U5 fixes: an oracle price exactly 300 s old now fails (D-151), `VAULT_IN_HANDOVER` replaces `EPOCH_MISMATCH` for agent swaps on a vault in handover (D-152), and the tool registry is documented in packages/domain and re-exported by packages/skills, with a test that they are the same objects (D-153). All earlier checks pass: lint, format, typecheck, forge build and non-fork tests, secrets scan (49 commits), doctor, dev:up, dev:status and test:fork. Note for the owner: `next dev` writes `apps/web/AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` when it detects an AI coding agent, with no opt-out; they are gitignored and were deleted, so decide whether you want them committed instead. Suggestions: add a `mobile` sheet component when a page needs side panels on phones; upload Playwright diffs as a CI artifact (a pinned upload action) on failure; FINAL_PLAN 4.4.2 still lacks `DEADLINE_TOO_FAR`; P0-U4 is next and must use these components.
Bugs: L-7, L-8, L-9.

## 2026-10-01, P0-U4, Dev console
Status: done
Summary: Moved the design system into `packages/ui` (apps/web's screenshots pass unchanged, pixel for pixel), extracted the scripts' local-environment logic into `packages/devenv` (RPC client, redaction, health, fork pin, and new: the local-fork guard, fork controls and test funds), and built `apps/console`, a separate local-only Next.js app from `packages/ui` with six panels: environment health and redacted config, fork controls (snapshot, revert, mine, advance time, reset to block 109670000 without handling the RPC secret), test funds (MON by anvil_setBalance, USDC minted through the real FiatToken contract by the impersonated master minter and a fork-only test minter), the address book, the policy sandbox (a preset per limit, each giving exactly its reason codes) and an agents empty state with extension points and the PB-U1 kill switch placeholder. Guards: the console refuses to start or build unless APP_ENV is local, binds only to 127.0.0.1 (checked with `ss`), answers only Host 127.0.0.1 or localhost (403 otherwise), and every state change first requires host 127.0.0.1, an `anvil/` client and chain 143, refusing a remote URL before any request. `pnpm dev:console`, `pnpm dev:all`, `pnpm test:console:e2e` and `pnpm test:console:live` are added. Results: 555 vitest tests with the fork up (the 8 fork integration tests skip cleanly without it), console screenshots and axe on all six pages at 1440px and 380px (28 Playwright tests), web e2e 6 of 6, lint, format, typecheck, forge build and non-fork tests, secrets scan (64 commits), doctor, dev:status and test:fork; a clean-checkout simulation of the CI web job, now also building and testing the console and uploading Playwright diffs on failure, passed in the pinned image. No secret appeared in any console page, the console log or `dev:all` output. Small fixes: FINAL_PLAN 4.4.2 lists DEADLINE_TOO_FAR, and the next dev agent files are ignored in every app. Not verified: `pnpm test:console:live` could not run here, because Docker Desktop on WSL2 does not share the distro's loopback with `--network=host`, and installing Playwright's browser on the host was not done without asking; the same flows were run against the stack through the console's devenv calls (block and time returned after revert, 42.5 MON and 1,234.56 USDC read back). BUILD_PLAN's P0-U4 row also asks to list agents from the database, trigger a no-op task and reset an agent; the prompt puts agents and schemas out of scope, so these are extension points for P1-U4, P1-U5 and P1-U8, and the row should be updated to say so. Suggestions: decide whether to install Playwright's Chromium on the host for the live spec; update the P0-U4 row in BUILD_PLAN.

## 2026-10-03, P1-U1, Hermes and E2B spike
Status: done (closed by D-163 on 2026-10-03; the unchecked items moved to W-1, Q-04 resolved by D-164, the L-19 sweep assigned to P1-U5 by D-165)
Summary: Hermes at 085d9ee runs as our brain in an E2B sandbox: `pnpm spike:hermes` started LiteLLM (pinned v1.103.2, `agent` compose profile, Postgres capped at 512m and LiteLLM at 1g), the stub tools server and the gate behind a Cloudflare quick tunnel, then ran one SCAN stage on claude-haiku-4-5 through a per-agent virtual key; the second run passed all eleven checks (`evidence/p1-u1/spike-2026-10-04T04-52-28.545Z.json`): non-allowlisted hosts fail (example.com, api.anthropic.com, 1.1.1.1), header injection authenticates at the gate, a forged gate header from the sandbox is overwritten, the skill mount is read-only, the run completes in 12.8 s with a schema-valid `complete_stage` that carries the skill's marker, replaying the idempotency key returns the same run with `Idempotency-Replayed`, no memory or skill writes, spend lands on the agent key (0.0139 USD, three log rows for three gateway calls), and no credential appears anywhere in the sandbox, including /run and every process environment. Q-04: E2B Secrets return 403 for this team, but a literal-value egress transform works, so the credential path is E2B injection (not the fallback proxy); the values sit in E2B's sandbox network config rather than its secrets store, and no second factor was chosen. Budget exhaustion: with a 1e-7 USD key, LiteLLM let the first call through (one call of overrun, 0.0045 USD), then answered 429 `budget_exceeded`; Hermes treated it as retryable, tried twice more, and failed the run in about 11 s with the LiteLLM message as its error, which is not H-10's target (end as `billing`, not retried, 402). Hermes booted in 12.7 s at about 320 MB RSS. WSL memory peaked at 2.4 GB used of 5.8 GB, with 3.5 GB still available. Not done in this unit: H-01 `hermes doctor`, H-04 `GET /v1/skills`, H-05 `hermes tools --summary`, H-09 `x-agent-id` on every request, H-12 tool absence, H-16, H-21, H-22, H-33, the B-04 marker leak table, MK-S1a `whoami`, and the in-sandbox script egress test as its own check (curl from the sandbox shell covers it in part). Suggestions: a startup sweep of tagged leftovers and key deletion in teardown (L-19); configure LiteLLM to return 402 on budget exhaustion and stop Hermes retrying it (H-10, P1-U6); budget checks must allow for a one-call overrun; pick the Q-04 second factor; `compose down` with the agent profile also stops the base Postgres and Redis.
Bugs: L-16, L-17, L-18, L-19.
Commit: 746e2ec, e7edfa9, 5152258, 66538e5, 7a2a33d, da1ab0b, 6b8fe08, plus the log commit.

## 2026-10-03, P2-U0, Venue and oracle spikes
Status: done
Summary: Step 0 recorded D-163 (P1-U1 done for the demo spine, its unchecked items moved to W-1), D-164 (Q-04: E2B egress-rule header injection accepted for the beta) and D-165 (the L-19 sweep and LiteLLM key deletion go to P1-U5), with BUILD_PLAN and the P1-U1 log entry updated. `pnpm spike:venues` measured Monad mainnet read-only at block 110390732, with 7 days of history; the report is `evidence/p2-u0/SUMMARY.md` with `data.json`.
- Q-01, recommended: the hookless Uniswap v4 MON/USDC 0.05% pool (ID 0x18a9...9954). It fits $22,120 buying and $9,743 selling inside 0.5% of Chainlink, never drifted more than 2% from the oracle, and a $10 or $200 trade would have passed every rule in 99.0% to 99.2% of minutes. It trades native MON, not WMON, so the P2-U2 adapter must wrap or the accounts must hold MON. Uniswap v3 0.3% (about $1.52M) is the fallback, at 84% to 86% because its fee takes 30 of the 50 basis points. Kuru (zero taker fee, 98.4% to 98.6%) is second.
- Q-02: MON/USD updates about every 30 s (p99 91 s, one 511 s gap) and meets 300 s 99.965% of the time. USDC/USD updates only hourly, so it needs 3,900 s and only drives the depeg guard. ETH/USD fails the 5-minute rule 17.5% of the time.
- Q-03: Circle lists 0x7547...b603 as Monad USDC. It is a FiatToken proxy with active pause and blacklist roles.
- Beta caps ($10 per trade, $200 burst) are far inside the depth of all three venues.
- The address book gains the v3 QuoterV2, the v4 Quoter and Kuru's Router, MarginAccount and MON-USDC market. Each is sourced to official docs and verified on the fork by `pnpm test:fork`. Sources may now be documentation URLs, and the console screenshots were re-baselined after a visual check.
- The USDC/USD feed address had an invalid checksum in the book and the plan, now fixed and guarded by a test.
- Lint, format, typecheck, 581 vitest tests, test:fork, console e2e 28 of 28, web e2e 6 of 6, forge build and the secrets scan pass.
Suggestions: decide Q-01, Q-02 and Q-03 and record them; make `oracleMaxAgeSeconds` per feed in packages/policy (FINAL_PLAN 4.1.9 already says per feed); re-quote in P2-U2 before pinning the adapter, and check the v4 pool's liquidity concentration; FINAL_PLAN and the asset model say WMON while the best pool and Kuru use native MON, which P2-U2 must settle; optionally tighten the 2% deviation rule to 1% (0.12% of minutes would be refused); the Permit2 route (TB-T04) is answered in passing, and fork fidelity and EIP-1153 remain for W-1; the configured RPC is Alchemy's free tier (10-block logs), so P2-U4's second provider (Q-23) should have wider log ranges.
Bugs: L-20, L-21, L-22, L-23, L-24, L-25, L-26, L-27.
Commit: 1c75397, bd9b534, ba07eb5, a54e678, de87bc7, a354f85, plus the log commit.

## 2026-10-04, P0-U7, Design tuning
Status: done
Summary: Step 0 recorded D-166 to D-171 (venue Uniswap v4 hookless MON/USDC 0.05% with v3 0.3% as fallback; accounts hold WMON with an atomic unwrap, swap and rewrap; per-feed staleness; the 2% rule stays; Q-03 resolved; a wide-log-range second RPC), with BUILD_PLAN and FINAL_PLAN updated. Q-02's spread and band parameters stay open.
- Tokens: the design system now matches the prototype. Every token change in `prototype-review.md` 2.3 is applied: cool graphite with a 950 page layer, flat 6px cards, the new shadow set, a 13px tabular body with a `2xs` step and label tracking, 3px to 8px radii, and motion tokens with `slot-pulse`, `status-pulse` and the reduced-motion rule.
- Contrast fixes (L-29): two values differ from the review because they failed AA where our components use them: red is #de6764 (proposed #d9534f) and ash-dim is #888c93 (proposed #6e727a). "Stale data" and "awaiting approval" now use the new amber warning tone.
- Fonts: Inter and JetBrains Mono load through `next/font/google` and are served from our origin, and a test in each app checks every font request is same-origin.
- Components: Button is 28, 36 and 40px with a `secondary-accent` variant, and inputs and selects are 36px to match. Tag and SectionLabel are new. `/design` shows every new token, the motion tokens and every new component in every state.
- Tabs fix (L-30): tab labels no longer spill out on mobile.
- Policy: per-feed staleness replaces the single oracle age: MON/USD 300 s and USDC/USD 3,900 s, both strictly under, with `PRICE_FEED` and `checkFeedAge` (tests at 299, 300, 301, 3,899, 3,900 and 3,901). The console sandbox now labels its field as the MON/USD age.
- Visual check and baselines: every web and console page was inspected at both widths before re-baselining. The baselines are in their own commit, and a one-unit brass change fails them (L-7).

## 2026-10-04, P1-U2, Privy login
Status: partial (built and tested against the mock wallet; the real Privy and MetaMask path has not run, because `.env` has no PRIVY_APP_ID or PRIVY_APP_SECRET)
Summary: Step 0 recorded D-172 to D-177, plus Q-42 to Q-45, in DECISIONS_AND_OPEN_QUESTIONS.md, and added the W-7 load and capacity session before PB-U1, renumbering the later rows of BUILD_PLAN section 4. The decisions:
- D-172: a 1,000-agent supply cap, enforced in the contract.
- D-173: free mint; the minter pays only gas. This supersedes the mint half of D-026.
- D-174: one agent per wallet, enforced in the contract.
- D-175: allowlist-only minting in the beta, with a claim gate open for public launch.
- D-176: revenue from the credit markup, skill sales and marketplace royalties; mint fees are not a revenue source.
- D-177: a design target of 100,000 users and 1,000 active agents, with no component assuming a single machine or process.
The open questions:
- Q-42: the public-launch claim gate.
- Q-43: how the 1,000 agents are split across tiers.
- Q-44: the E2B plan's concurrent sandbox limit.
- Q-45: whether one agent per wallet also limits holding, which would affect buying through the escrow.
The unit:
- Login: the app shell's wallet button is real. It uses Privy with MetaMask only (no embedded wallets; OKX stays deferred to W-6) and wagmi on the one chain the build targets, which `APP_CHAINS` in packages/config picks from APP_ENV. Browsers use Monad's public RPCs or the local fork, never a keyed URL.
- Login states: WalletButton and WrongChainPrompt cover logged out, connecting, wrong chain, connected and error. Both are in packages/ui and on /design.
- Wrong chain: the shell shows the switch prompt and makes the page inert and dimmed until the wallet switches. `WalletSession.ready` gates later actions.
- Server check: `GET /api/session` verifies a Privy access token with @privy-io/node. It answers 200 with the user and session, 401 for a missing or invalid token, or 503 when Privy is not configured. The secret is read only on the server.
- Mock wallet: test builds swap in a mock wallet only with `ALPHA_E2E_MOCK_WALLET=1` on local, into a separate `.next-e2e`, labeled "Test build: mock wallet". `scripts/check-web-build.js`, now run by `test:web:e2e` and CI, proves the real build holds neither the mock nor a sentinel app secret.
- Checks: lint, format, typecheck, 699 vitest tests, web e2e 30 of 30, console e2e 30 of 30, the build check, test:fork, forge build and the secrets scan pass.
Notes for the owner:
- Setup: to test with MetaMask, add a Privy app's 25-character PRIVY_APP_ID and PRIVY_APP_SECRET to `.env`, enable wallet login with MetaMask, and allow http://localhost:3000 in the Privy dashboard.
- Local fork caveat: the local fork uses chain ID 143, the same as Monad mainnet, so the app cannot tell a wallet pointed at mainnet from one pointed at the fork. For local testing, MetaMask's chain-143 network must use http://127.0.0.1:8545.
- Unmet peer dependencies: Privy pulls Solana packages that expect TypeScript 5 (we have 6; types only, never imported, `skipLibCheck` is on), and an old `use-sync-external-store` that lists React 18 at most (a shim that defers to React 19's built-in hook). Both are inside Privy's dependency tree and are left as they are.
- viem versions: apps/web uses viem 2.56.5, which @privy-io/wagmi pins exactly; the root stays on 2.57.2.
Suggestions:
- BUILD_PLAN's P1-U2 row also asks for an API session bound to current ownership and epoch. That belongs to the API unit (P1-U4) and should be moved there.
- The header shows both the app's environment chip and the wallet's chain chip; a later shell pass could merge them.
Bugs: L-31, L-32, L-33, L-34.
Commit: 6a802b4, 5e5b3cd, 8717d31, 14111d4, 240b94b, 21182e2, plus the log commit.

## 2026-10-04, P1-U2, Privy login diagnosis and fixes
Status: done (the login code path now works up to the MetaMask step; a real MetaMask login still needs the owner's browser and a real app secret)
Summary: The owner saw "Login failed" with a "Try again" that did nothing.
- Findings in .env: PRIVY_APP_ID is 25 characters, lowercase and digits, as Privy requires. PRIVY_APP_SECRET is 40 characters, but 36 of them are asterisks: it is the dashboard's masked copy. Privy's API rejects it with 401 "Invalid app ID or app secret.", the same as a deliberately wrong control secret, so every real login would fail server-side.
- Root cause of the button: `pnpm dev:web` never loaded the root .env (L-35), so the app ran as not configured and /api/session answered 503. That state showed a misleading "Login failed" with a dead button (L-36). Both are fixed (9538cf9, 4dd673f).
- With the fix: /api/session answers 401 (configured), the button reads "Connect wallet", and clicking it opens Privy's "Log in or sign up" modal with MetaMask, with no console errors and every auth.privy.io request answering 200 on localhost.
- 127.0.0.1: Privy never started, and the dev server refused its live-reload connection, so use localhost.
- The wallet button is in the shell header on every route, including the unbuilt ones that return 404.
- Checks: lint, format, typecheck, 700 vitest tests, web e2e 32 of 32, console e2e 30 of 30 and the secrets scan pass.
- The owner must paste the full app secret into .env and use http://localhost:3000.
Bugs: L-35, L-36.
Commit: 9538cf9, 4dd673f, plus the log commit.

## 2026-10-04, P1-U3, AgentNFT
Status: done (local fork). Testnet is not deployed, because `.env` has no MONAD_TESTNET_RPC_URL or TESTNET_DEPLOYER_PRIVATE_KEY.
Summary: Step 0 recorded D-178 to D-186 and resolved Q-42, Q-43 and Q-45:
- Tiers: 600 base, 300 medium, 100 pro, assigned randomly at reveal.
- Species: 25 with exact counts, listed in `packages/domain`.
- Mint: two-step, free, one mint per wallet, with signed claims and an admin switch for claim-required mode.
- Metadata and royalties: onchain metadata with a freezable image link, 3D models offchain, 5% royalties, and "Alpha Agent #<id>" as the token name.
D-187 records the randomness choice: Pyth Entropy v2 with batch reveals.
- Research: Entropy is deployed on Monad mainnet and testnet and verified on the fork. Chainlink VRF is not on Monad. `blockhash` and `prevrandao` are known to, or chosen by, Monad's block leader (`evidence/p1-u3/RANDOMNESS.md`).
- Contract: `chains/monad/src/AgentNFT.sol` (OpenZeppelin 5.7.0 through Soldeer).
  - Supply: a packed deck of exact counts, drawn uniformly at reveal.
  - Minting: `mint` (open mode) and `mintWithClaim` (EIP-712, with expiry, single-use nonce and wallet binding).
  - Accounts: the Tokenbound account is created and initialized at mint. The implementation slot is checked, so a front-run creation cannot block a mint.
  - Reveal: `requestReveal` (anyone, pays the fee) and a callback that only stores the number. `reveal(maxCount)` lets anyone apply it in mint order. One request is pending at a time, and a re-request is allowed only after an hour with no delivery.
  - Transfers: escrow-only (the escrow is set once). They never go to an agent account, and each one bumps the epoch. There is no burn.
  - Metadata: tokenURI is JSON built onchain. ERC-2981 royalties at 5%, ERC-4906 events.
  - Admin powers: exactly the listed ones, and renounce is disabled.
- Tests: 67 unit, fuzz, invariant and attack tests. These include minting and revealing all 1,000 under random randomness with exact counts, invariants over random sequences with stale and repeated deliveries, and an ABI check that the state-changing functions are exactly the intended set. Eight fork tests cover spikes TB-1, TB-2, TB-3, TB-5, TB-8, TB-9b and TB-9c against the real registry, account and Entropy.
- Account addresses: `tokenboundAccountAddress` in packages/domain equals @tokenbound/sdk and the registry's `account()` on the fork.
- Deploy: `pnpm deploy:agent-nft` (deterministic CREATE2 with a state assertion; local, or testnet with credentials; mainnet refused), and `pnpm agent-nft:local mint | reveal | show <id>`.
- Address book: gains Pyth Entropy and the local AgentNFT (`0x08e05A0e5400CcF847f1B94A49b35E75C2f8DAf9`, verified for local only). The console's address book screenshots were re-baselined after a visual check.
- Gas (`evidence/p1-u3/GAS.md`): a claim mint is about 334,000 gas, a reveal about 20,000 per agent, and a request about 175,000 plus the 1.4 MON fee.
- Checks: lint, format, typecheck, 713 vitest tests, forge build, 67 forge tests, test:fork (11 tests and the address book), console e2e 30 of 30, and the secrets scan pass. Web e2e passed 32 of 32 on three runs; one earlier run had a 1-pixel difference in `shell-wrong-chain.png` (a P1-U2 test this unit did not touch).
- Security note: a probe printed anvil's node info, which includes the upstream RPC URL with its key, into the session output (L-38). Nothing was written or committed. The owner may want to rotate that key.
Suggestions:
- Claims: the API claim signer service has no owning unit yet (PB-U1 needs it); P1-U4 is the natural home.
- Reveal keeper: something must call `requestReveal` and `reveal` after mints (the orchestrator in P1-U5, or a small keeper), and fund the 1.4 MON fee per batch.
- 3D models: P1-U11 still says "a base body per tier". With 25 species (D-182) the owner should decide whether there are 25 models or one body per tier mapped from species.
- Images: upload the 25 species images and `unrevealed.png` to IPFS, then `setImageBaseURI` and `freezeImageBaseURI`.
- Admin: make it a TimelockController or Safe at mainnet deployment (PB-U1). The contract has no built-in delay, so D-145's "only the timelock can disable" holds only if the admin is a timelock.
- Before any mainnet deployment, since the contract cannot change afterwards: decide whether to add a collection-level `contractURI`. Also note that the runtime code is 32,759 bytes (fine on Monad's 128 KB limit; any port to another EVM must split it).
- Residual risks: the Entropy provider could withhold a callback and force a re-request after an hour (provider trust, not a minter path). If Tokenbound's Safe ever trusts a second implementation (Q-14), someone could pre-initialize the next agent's account with it and block minting.
- Testnet: add MONAD_TESTNET_RPC_URL and a funded TESTNET_DEPLOYER_PRIVATE_KEY to `.env`, then run `pnpm deploy:agent-nft testnet` and record the address in the book.
- Flake: watch `shell-wrong-chain.png` for repeated 1-pixel flakes.
Bugs: L-37, L-38, L-39, L-40, L-41, L-42.
Commit: 898a37c, acf1482, 35a03d8, d8645c8, b7e4092, 879a941, 5c19f9f, bc7e180, 6676ec2, 05c559e, dc38186, plus the log commit.

## 2026-10-05, P1-U11 (fix after handoff), Local fork chain ID and wallet network guard
Status: done
Summary: The owner's playtest mint went to Monad mainnet: the fork shared chain ID 143 with mainnet, and MetaMask, with no MON on chain 143, sent it through its gasless relay, which also set an EIP-7702 delegation on the owner's mainnet account (L-53). D-195 records the fix.
- Feasibility first: a probe anvil with `--chain-id 143143 --network monad` reported network monad and hardfork MonadTen. forge forked it under `network = "monad"` and deployed a 30,000-byte contract, which only Monad's size limit allows; a plain Ethereum anvil refused the same deploy with CreateContractSizeLimit. 143143 is in none of the 2,792 chains on chainid.network.
- Chain ID (56d4df0): `LOCAL_FORK_CHAIN_ID` in packages/config sets the local environment's chain, so the wagmi chain, the claim route's EIP-712 domain and the mock wallet follow it. The anvil runner passes `--chain-id` and `--network monad`. The local-fork guard, dev:status and a new doctor check expect 143143 and tell an old fork on 143 to restart. The deploy script accepts the fork or testnet, not mainnet. The reveal simulator seeds with 143143, AgentNFT's local address book entry is verified on 143143 (mainnet entries stay 143, the state the fork copies, and `fork.json` keeps 143 as the chain copied), and the forge fork tests bind accounts to block.chainid. A new fork smoke test checks Monad's contract size limit. The console's environment and address book captures show 143143.
- Network guard (789af6d): before a claim is requested, the app compares the wallet's latest block and AgentNFT's code, read through the wallet's own provider, with the app's RPC, and stops with the wallet's network named.
- Named failure (4c49dab): a mint the app's RPC never sees is looked up on the wallet's network and reported as sent to a different network, not as failed.
- Docs: D-195; L-53 and L-54; the README's new MetaMask section and environment tables.
- Checks: lint, format, typecheck, 838 vitest tests (none skipped, fork up), test:fork (12 forge fork tests and the address book on 143143), forge 81 of 81, the web build check and web e2e 59 passed (1 desktop-only skip), the live suite 4 of 4 on 143143 (twice, before and after the receipt change), console e2e 30 of 30, and the secrets scan pass.
Notes for the owner:
- Mainnet account: the EIP-7702 delegation on 0x683e...5f76 to 0x63c0c19a...e32b stays until removed in MetaMask. It holds 0 MON on mainnet now. Check the delegate on an explorer, and switch the account back to a regular account if the upgrade was not intended.
- The fork was restarted as 143143, which cleared its state; redeploy with `pnpm deploy:agent-nft`.
- `.env` has Windows line endings (CRLF). The project's loader reads it correctly, but a shell `source .env` does not.
- Commit 56d4df0 is tagged `[D-195]` instead of the unit ID `[P1-U11]`; history is not rewritten.
Suggestions:
- L-51 (the anvil_reset fork cache bug) now fails about every other reset; a later unit could try `--no-storage-caching` or a newer Foundry.
- The network guard runs before minting only; P1-U4 and later units that send transactions should call `checkWalletNetwork` too.
Bugs: L-53, L-54.
Commit: 56d4df0, 789af6d, 4c49dab, plus the docs commit.

## 2026-10-05, P1-U11 (fix after handoff), Network switch buttons
Status: done (verified with the mock wallet and unit tests; a real MetaMask switch still needs the owner's browser)
Summary: The owner reported that both switch buttons did nothing and no MetaMask prompt appeared, while the app said the wallet was on Monad Testnet. The console output in the request was the placeholder, so the diagnosis is from the code and the installed Privy and wagmi sources.
- Registration: chain 143143 was already in the wagmi chain list and in Privy's `defaultChain` and `supportedChains` (all from `appChain(environment)`); nothing there was missed.
- Cause (L-55): the buttons fired wagmi's `switchChain` and dropped its result, and wagmi's injected connector waits for a chainChanged event that may never come. The app's chain was wagmi's cached connection chain, which can differ from MetaMask's per-site network. Privy's own switch path would also add an unknown chain with an empty explorer URL. The mock wallet switched instantly, so none of this was tested.
- Fix: `switchWalletChain` (apps/web/src/auth/switch-chain.ts) asks the wallet's provider to switch, adds the chain on 4902 (also -32603 wrapping 4902) with the fork's RPC, chain ID and MON, switches again, and confirms with eth_chainId. WrongChainPrompt shows the outcome: waiting in the wallet, declined, a request already open, failed with the wallet's error, or still on the old network with how to fix MetaMask's per-site network; a switch shows a toast. `useWalletChainId` reads the chain from the provider on connect, chainChanged and window focus and moves wagmi's connection to it.
- Design system: WrongChainPrompt's status line and a "Network switch outcomes" specimen on /design, added first (e28f012).
- Tests: 10 unit tests for the switch (approve, unknown chain, 4902 wrapped as -32603, declined switch, declined add, request already open, add error, other error, accepted but still on the old network, add parameters); 13 end-to-end tests with the mock wallet answering as MetaMask can (two approve paths and four failures at both widths, and the header button on desktop).
- Checks: lint, format, typecheck, 851 vitest tests on the committed tree (twice), and web e2e 72 passed with 2 skipped (desktop-only tests on mobile), after a visual check of the two changed /design captures.
- Process: c07da24's gate passed through a grep pipeline while one staged test (the L-51 anvil reset) had failed; the committed tree was rechecked and passes (L-56). One web e2e run overlapped the owner's running `next dev`, against the Heavy work rule.
Notes for the owner:
- MetaMask can keep a network per site. If the app still shows another network, open MetaMask while on localhost:3000 and choose Monad (local fork) for this site, or press the switch button: the prompt now says what happened.
Bugs: L-55, L-56.
Commit: e28f012, c07da24, plus the log commit.

## 2026-10-05, P1-U11 (fix after handoff), Wallet network guard false positive
Status: done (the guard passes on the real fork in the live suite; an OKX check still needs the owner's browser)
Summary: With OKX correctly on the local fork, the guard blocked the mint with "Your wallet is on a network that reports chain 143143, not Monad (local fork)".
- Diagnosis (L-57): the old check failed in one of two places that gave the same text: the wallet's "latest" block against the app's block at that number (unreliable: wallets cache "latest", and a block from the earlier fork run has another hash), or a wallet answer to eth_getCode that was an error, counted as a mismatch. Which one OKX hit cannot be told from the text, which was itself the defect.
- Fix (ead099e): the chain ID through the wallet's provider must equal the app's; the pinned block's hash must be the same through the wallet and the app; AgentNFT must have code through the wallet. The pinned block's hash is identical on the fork and Monad mainnet (checked through both RPCs: 0x2ea4dc88...), so the chain ID and code checks catch mainnet. Each failure names what failed; a silent wallet is reported as silent; a missing deployment on the app's side says to run pnpm deploy:agent-nft.
- Wallet calls: every one goes through the connected connector's provider (walletRequest); nothing in apps/web or packages/ui uses window.ethereum.
- AgentNFT: deployed on the running fork (34,459 bytes at 0x60cacA6dE327331b321E140Ae19AcbCc4188Be6E). It was missing before the live run because this task's `pnpm test` reset the fork (L-58); the live suite's setup redeployed it.
- Tests: 9 guard unit tests, including a stale latest block on the right network (passes), Monad mainnet on 143 (fails on the chain ID) and 143143 on another node (fails on the pinned block); the portal guard test at both widths.
- Checks: lint, format, typecheck and 853 vitest tests before the final comment-only edit (the unit suite was not rerun after it, to avoid resetting the owner's fork again); web e2e 72 passed with 2 skipped (desktop-only); the live mint, reveal and view suite 4 of 4 on the running fork. With the owner's approval, their `pnpm dev:web` was stopped first, so the heavy suites ran one at a time with no dev server.
Suggestions:
- Make the fork integration test's reset restore the fork it found (L-58).
Bugs: L-57, L-58 (found, not fixed).
Commit: ead099e, plus the log commit.

## 2026-10-05, P1-U10, Mint page (mint half only)
Status: done (local fork). The landing page and its counters stay cut from the beta (D-160).
Summary: /mint shows the supply and the three tiers with their odds, all read from AgentNFT, and mints through P1-U11's flow.
- Design system first (ac1419b): TierCard (slots, supply, remaining with a bar, chance per mint, every species with a thumbnail and its remaining count, one-of-ones marked "1 of 1" with "Drawn" or "Not drawn yet", a sold-out tag and a loading state) and MintPanel (unavailable, logged out, connecting, wrong network, checking, read error, ready, already minted, sold out, waiting for reveal, revealed), both on /design. SpeciesArt gains a compact, decorative thumbnail.
- Page (ade5086): `readSupply` reads MAX_SUPPLY, totalMinted and remainingOf for all 25 species through the app's chain client every 10 seconds and after a mint; `summarizeSupply` gives each tier's remaining count and its odds (its remaining slots over all remaining slots, which is the next mint's chance because each reveal draws uniformly from what is left). The deck shrinks at reveal, so the supply card also shows minted agents waiting for their reveal. `useWalletMint` reads hasMinted and the wallet's AgentMinted event; `mintPageView` picks the panel state. The mint button is P1-U11's flow, whose network guard runs before the claim. Mint is in the nav. Tier totals come from packages/domain's species counts, which species.test.ts checks against the contract source; every other number on screen is read from the chain.
- Fix (f10aea3, L-61): the button now follows the panel when the page's own read sees the reveal first.
- Header (fb20706, L-62): Mint made the connected desktop header 29px too wide; nav links use 8px side padding.
- Tests: 17 supply and odds unit tests (fresh deck, a revealed pro, pending reveals, a sold-out tier, the whole supply sold out with and without pending reveals, one slot left, bad input, formatting) and 22 panel state tests; 26 component tests for TierCard, compact SpeciesArt and MintPanel. mint.spec.ts (3e03e4a): nine states captured and axe-scanned at 1440px and 380px (logged out, ready, wrong network, no claim, minting, waiting for reveal, revealed, already minted, sold out), remaining counts and odds against the fake chain's numbers, a sold-out tier, the network guard stopping a wallet on another node before the claim, and the nav link. FakeChain now serves the supply reads and AgentMinted logs, takes the mock wallet's mint and can hold its receipt. The live suite (b5c5b9b) checks the page's minted count, tier remaining counts and odds against direct contract reads, mints, checks the owner and the new count, reveals, follows the link to /configure, and sees "already minted" on a second visit.
- Checks on fb20706: format, lint, typecheck, 928 vitest tests (twice), assets:check, secrets scan; web e2e 94 passed with 2 desktop-only skips (twice, after one `--update` run whose four changed baselines were inspected: the header row and the new /design specimens); console e2e 30 of 30; the live suite 5 of 5 (four runs). `test:fork` fails its address book step on the owner's fork (L-63, found, not fixed) and passes on a fresh fork, run inside a dump, reset and load that left the fork as found. Every heavy suite ran alone with no dev server, after checking free memory.
- Fork: as found at the start (head 109670005, agent 1 the bee owned by 0x683e...5f76, the same balance), checked after every heavy run.
Suggestions:
- Restore a shared fork without losing its history (L-63): reset to the pin and replay the recorded transactions, or run tests that need a fresh deck on a second anvil port instead of resetting the dev fork.
- MintPanel's agent link is a plain anchor, so it reloads the page. A Next Link through `asChild` from the app would keep client navigation; the portal's agent switcher does the same.
- The page makes 27 eth_calls per supply read. On a public testnet RPC, batch them through Multicall3 or add one view to AgentNFT in a later contract revision.

## 2026-10-05, P1-U4, Indexer and API
Status: done (local fork), with one check that fails on its first run after other fork tests (L-70). Testnet and mainnet have no AgentNFT deployment yet, so the indexer and API there run but report "not deployed".
Summary: A log-poller indexer and a control API now serve the app from Postgres, and the real claim signer with a database allowlist replaces P1-U11's local route. Fresh-chain tests run on forks of their own.
- Decisions (4bfb5ba): D-197 our own RPC log poller, not Envio: Envio guarantees reorg detection only with HyperSync, the local fork is RPC only, and Envio owns its schema. D-198 the claim signer in the control API, key from configuration until KMS before the beta. D-199 Kysely migrations and Hono. D-200 fresh-chain tests on their own anvil.
- Config (be14705): LOCAL_FORK_PORT, CLAIM_SIGNER_PRIVATE_KEY (renamed from LOCAL_CLAIM_SIGNER_PRIVATE_KEY), API_SESSION_SECRET, CONTROL_API_PORT and CONTROL_API_URL.
- Database (3072750): packages/db, the first migration (indexer schema: watermarks, indexed block hashes, raw AgentNFT events, agents, USDC transfers, incidents; platform schema: mint allowlist, issued claims), pnpm db:migrate, db:reset (local and loopback only), and pnpm allowlist add/remove/list.
- Indexer (246d65f): all 20 AgentNFT events stored raw (a test compares the list with forge inspect), the agents projection, and USDC transfers into and out of agents' token-bound accounts. Each range commits with its watermark; every record has its block number and hash; the watermark block's hash is checked before each range, and a reorg or rewind rolls back to the newest stored block the chain still has, rebuilds the agents and records an incident. Rate limits back off; refused ranges are halved (L-21). pnpm dev:indexer.
- Control API (b8b6168): supply, agents by owner or minter, and agent by ID from the index with the watermark, cached for a second, with no contract reads; Privy sessions; owner sessions bound to wallet and ownership epoch and rechecked on chain at every use; mint eligibility and the claim (session, linked wallet, allowlist, fresh hasMinted, supply). It refuses to start with the mock identity outside local or the local session secret elsewhere, and says so if the claim key does not match AgentNFT's claimSigner. pnpm dev:api.
- Fork isolation (6f5b36d): startTestFork runs the fork on another port with a redacted log and a PID file that a later run uses to stop a leftover. The devenv fork test and pnpm test:fork run on it, so test:fork's address book check passes again (its L-63 failure was the playtest fork's).
- Web (be04bf0): /mint and /configure read from the API; "not eligible" shows before the click (new MintPanel state, on /design first); the claim comes from the API; the reveal is watched in the index; "Open agent" uses Next's Link. The web dev claim route, session route and server code are gone. The dev console lists agents from the API.
- Tests: 21 indexer unit tests on an in-memory chain (catch-up, block hashes on every record, restart with no gap or duplicate, reorg to the common ancestor and below every stored block, rewind, transfers, range splitting, idle cost) plus 4 on a real fork of its own (catch-up checked against ownerOf, speciesOf and tbaOf, restart, a real reorg made with evm_revert, a rewind); 24 API tests covering every route, including not allowlisted, already minted, sold out, invalid and missing sessions, a wallet linked to someone else, a stale owner session after a transfer, CORS and no leak in an error; config, db, console and panel-state tests. The screenshot suite runs on a fake API (95fc33f) and captures "not eligible" and "no claim". The console's agents panel is captured against a fixture API (97e524a). The live suite (pnpm test:web:live) runs on its own stack: fork 8546, a throwaway database, the indexer and the API on 4101. It mints through the API claim, follows the client-side link, is refused when off the allowlist (before the click and at the claim), and finds the signer key in no service log or page; the indexer rolled back every test's revert.
- Checks on the final tree: format, lint, typecheck, assets:check and secrets scan; 959 vitest tests (none skipped, with the stack up); forge 69 of 69; web e2e 96 passed with 2 desktop-only skips; console e2e 30 of 30; the live suite 7 of 7 (three runs); test:fork 12 of 12 run on its own, twice. Run right after other fork tests, test:fork's first run fails test_GasForMintAndReveal on a fork-fetch timeout (L-70). Heavy suites ran one at a time after checking memory; another program used up to 29% of memory during the session.
- Dev state: the playtest fork is as found (head 109670005, agent 1 the bee owned by 0x683e...5f76, the same balance) and was only read: the indexer indexed it into the development database, which now holds the migrations and the bee. No test database or test fork was left running.
Notes for the owner:
- Rename LOCAL_CLAIM_SIGNER_PRIVATE_KEY to CLAIM_SIGNER_PRIVATE_KEY in .env; the API refuses to start with the old name and says so.
- af4de72 also carries the deletion of the web dev claim route, staged earlier (L-66), so between af4de72 and be04bf0 the web mint posts to a route that no longer exists. History is not rewritten.
Suggestions:

- CI has no Postgres, so the db, indexer and API tests skip there; add a Postgres service to the CI test job.

## 2026-10-05, P1-U5, Agent provisioning (thin)
Status: done (local fork). Out of scope as the unit prompt says: credits and funding addresses (P1-U6), real tool servers (P1-U7), state export and restore, token rotation, any testnet or mainnet deployment. BUILD_PLAN's row also names a KMS funding address key; that moves with P1-U6.
Summary: A revealed agent is now set up to run automatically, reveals happen through a batching keeper, and the dev console runs one agent end to end.
- Decisions (878cc74): D-201 the keeper's policy (a 10 second window locally, 60 seconds elsewhere, chunks of 50, simulated Entropy delivery only on the fork), D-202 the orchestrator's records and queue, D-203 per-lease gate tokens with the virtual key kept out of E2B, D-204 the config layers, D-205 the loopback API with local-only write routes. D-206 (8bad7c2) records the L-70 choice.
- Config and schema (dbab3e0): ORCHESTRATOR_PORT, ORCHESTRATOR_URL, ORCHESTRATOR_SECRET, REVEAL_KEEPER_PRIVATE_KEY; LITELLM_BASE_URL defaults to the local gateway. Migration 0002: platform.agent_runtimes, platform.sandbox_leases (one active lease per agent by a partial unique index), platform.agent_tasks.
- Config renderer (e0632c3): base template from FINAL_PLAN 4.3.2 (terminal, code execution and file now on, in /workspace), tier overlays (3, 5, 8 slots; tier-base@0 and the others; two strategy playbook skills; starter workflows per A-12) and agent overrides, validated by a schema that locks every containment setting, hashed when stored. The rendered config.yaml is JSON; a test reads it with the pinned Hermes's own hermes_yaml reader.
- Provisioning (12ee0d2): a reconciler over the indexer's agents enqueues deterministic BullMQ jobs; provisioning stores the encrypted key, checks LiteLLM for the alias, then creates the key with a 1 USD starting budget; deprovisioning ends the lease, deletes the key and confirms it is gone; reset makes a new generation.
- Keeper (f2cd946): requestReveal after the window, simulated delivery on the fork, reveal in chunks, re-request after REVEAL_TIMEOUT elsewhere; a fork test on its own fork (8549) shows three mints sharing one request.
- Service (76258d0): the gate (the lease's token in, the agent's own LiteLLM key out, forged headers dropped), the tunnel with a PID file, the startup sweep (tunnel, tagged sandboxes, leases and their tokens, running tasks, orphan keys), the no-op task, the internal API, `pnpm dev:orchestrator` and `pnpm dev:litellm`.
- Live check (2f7cede): `pnpm test:orchestrator:live` on its own fork (8550), database, namespace and API port. Its first run passed 11 of 11: mint to provisioned with no manual step in 19 seconds; config tier matches the chain; the no-op task answered NOOP_OK through the gate in 32 seconds (sandbox 0.8 s, Hermes boot 13.7 s, run 10.2 s, 2 model calls) and stopped its sandbox; a SIGKILL during a second task left a sandbox and a live tunnel, and the restarted orchestrator's sweep removed both, ended the lease and failed the task; reset replaced the key; no secret in 79 log lines and replies. Report: evidence/p1-u5/.
- L-70 (8bad7c2): the gas test mints 10 agents instead of 50, and test:fork retries once on a fresh fork for a classified transient fetch error. Both paths have now run: cold runs after the keeper's fork test passed in 10 to 14 seconds, and the final run after the full suite timed out in two tests first and passed 12 of 12 on the retry.
- CI (6742102): Postgres and Redis services on the test job, and a step that fails if the database tests report any skip (checked both ways locally). Not yet seen running in GitHub Actions, since nothing is pushed.
- UI: RuntimeStatusBadge and TaskResult on /design first (812682f); the console's agents panel shows runtimes, runs the no-op task with a live result and resets with a confirmation (4afc875); the mint panel and portal say the keeper reveals the agent (12f8491). Every changed capture was looked at at both widths; two layout issues were fixed before baselining (a command wrapping mid-name, result values breaking mid-word).
- Checks on the final tree: format, lint, typecheck, secrets scan; 1,032 vitest tests (none skipped, stack up); forge 69 of 69; test:fork 12 of 12 (after one retry, above); web e2e 96 passed with 2 desktop-only skips; console e2e 36 of 36; the web live suite 7 of 7; the orchestrator live check 11 of 11. Heavy suites ran one at a time with no dev server, after checking memory. Another session's Python job held up to 3.4 GiB for part of the session, so the Playwright suites waited until memory was free.

## 2026-10-05, P1-U6, Credits (thin)
Status: done (local fork). Out of scope as the unit prompt says: metering sandbox minutes and gas, gas top-ups, the settlement sweep, KMS, the My Agents page (P1-U9), testnet and mainnet.
Summary: Each agent has a funding address; USDC sent to it becomes credits with no other step; model calls are metered against them; at zero the agent's LLM work stops cleanly as billing while deterministic work continues; the owner can take the credits back; `pnpm dev:all` starts everything.
- Decisions (b94e519): D-207 funding addresses derived from `FUNDING_ADDRESS_SEED` (KMS before the beta), D-208 the double-entry ledger, metering and budget sync, D-209 zero credits as a 402 Hermes does not retry, D-210 refunds bound to the owner and ownership epoch, D-211 dev:all and dev:down. Assumptions A-27 (provider cost plus 25% while Q-09 is open) and A-28 (a 50 USDC beta cap, the excess held and refunded).
- Accounting (6abdd99): agent-scoped ledger accounts (funding address, credits, held deposits, unsettled usage) whose lines must name their agent; pure builders for deposit, usage, refund and reversal entries that pass the journal's double-entry check; the price table, the cap split and the key budget formula.
- Schema (4e247ea): `FUNDING_ADDRESS_SEED`; migration 0003 (funding addresses, ledger entries and lines, usage receipts, refunds with one open per agent, the account kind on indexed USDC transfers). A random local seed was added to `.env` without printing it.
- Indexer (db08299): watches funding addresses like token-bound accounts and marks each transfer's side.
- Orchestrator (e996526, 60296bc, 3f5b50e): records a funding address for every indexed agent; credits each indexed deposit once (keyed by transaction, log and block hash), up to the cap with the rest held and flagged, and reverses one a reorg removes; meters each costed LiteLLM request once; keeps each key's budget at its metered spend plus what the credits buy, and gives a new key the budget its credits buy (zero when unfunded); meters a key's last calls before a reset deletes it; refuses LLM tasks at zero; refunds after rechecking owner and epoch on chain, storing the signed transfer with its ledger entry before broadcasting. The gate answers 402 `insufficient_quota` at zero and turns LiteLLM's 429 `budget_exceeded` into the same 402. Gas for a refund comes from anvil_setBalance on the local fork only.
- API (7034028): `GET /v1/agents/:id/credits`; `POST /v1/agents/:id/credits/refund` behind the owner session and a fresh chain read (a stale epoch is refused), sharing one owner check with `/owner`; the refund's status.
- dev:all (503a408, 88eda2c): one supervised process for the stack, LiteLLM, indexer, API, orchestrator, web and console, with labeled combined logs in the terminal and `.dev/dev-all.log`; it refuses while a heavy suite runs or with under 2 GiB available; dev:down stops its services by PID, then anvil and every container, LiteLLM included.
- Console (a6858ca): funding address under the owner, spendable credits with any held amount flagged, 24-hour spend to four decimals, RESTRICTED through StatusPill, "Fund 5 USDC" and "Refund" with a confirmation and a followed status. Every changed capture was looked at at both widths; two layout problems were fixed first (L-80).
- Live check (1c55146): `pnpm test:orchestrator:live` passed 18 of 18 on its own fork, database and namespace with a real model and E2B: zero credits refuse a task (409); 1 USDC sent to the funding address is credited in 6.4 s with the key budget set to 0.8 USD; the no-op task's charge (11,225 USDC units, one costed request) equals LiteLLM's costed rows and the ledger balances; the hard kill and sweep from P1-U5; a reset; a refund of 988,775 units paid to the owner on chain; with the credits spent while a task waited, its only completion got a 402 and the run ended as billing with no retry; while restricted, a second agent was revealed and provisioned and a deposit was credited; the ledger's funding address matched the chain (3,013,225 units); no secret in 170 log lines and replies. Report: evidence/p1-u6/.
- dev:all was tried twice on the dev stack: all five services started and answered (agents page 200, agent 1 provisioned with its funding address, RESTRICTED), and stopping by PID freed every port. dev:down itself was not run, because it stops anvil and anvil keeps no state (the playtest fork would be lost); its container step was checked with `docker compose --profile agent down --dry-run`, which lists LiteLLM, Postgres and Redis.
- Checks on the final tree: format, lint, typecheck, secrets scan; 1,067 vitest tests (none skipped, stack up); forge 69 of 69; test:fork 12 of 12 after its classified retry fired once; web e2e 96 passed with 2 desktop-only skips; console e2e 40 of 40; the web live suite 7 of 7; the orchestrator live check 18 of 18. Heavy suites ran one at a time with no dev server, after checking memory.
- Dev state: the playtest fork is as found (head 109670005, same hash, agent 1 the bee, same owner balance). The development database gained migration 0003, agent 1's runtime (P1-U5's provisioning, first run here by dev:all) and its funding address 0xF1F1...11c4, with a LiteLLM key of budget 0. LiteLLM is stopped again, as found. No test database, fork, tunnel or E2B sandbox is left.
- Process: e996526 was committed while its gate had failed (L-77); the failing test was fixed in 60296bc. db08299 carries the co-author trailer twice. History is not rewritten.
Notes for the owner:
- `.env` gained `FUNDING_ADDRESS_SEED`, a random local seed; keep it out of every other environment and set a different one per environment.
- `pnpm dev:down` loses the fork's state, as it always has; redeploy with `pnpm deploy:agent-nft` after `pnpm dev:up`.
- Agents now start with zero credits, so the no-op task needs funding first ("Fund 5 USDC" in the console).
Suggestions:
- The settlement sweep (W-1), gas top-ups and sandbox and gas metering (W-5) remain from BUILD_PLAN's P1-U6 row.
- A funding address recorded after a deposit already happened is not backfilled by the indexer; addresses are recorded within a second of the mint, before anyone can see them, so this cannot happen today, but a backfill would make it robust.
- Metering polls LiteLLM's spend log for every provisioned agent every two seconds; at more than a few dozen agents, use LiteLLM's spend webhooks or a longer interval.
- Held deposits are released only by a refund; an operator release (or automatic release as credits fall below the cap) is a small follow-up.
- `pnpm dev:all` could persist anvil's state with `--dump-state`/`--load-state` so dev:down stops losing the fork.
- The console's agents table still scrolls sideways at 380px.
Bugs: L-76, L-77, L-78, L-79, L-80, L-81.
Commit: b94e519, 6abdd99, 4e247ea, db08299, e996526, 60296bc, 7034028, 503a408, 3f5b50e, a6858ca, 1c55146, 88eda2c, plus the docs commit.

## 2026-10-06, P1-U7, Tool servers and narrator (thin)
Status: done (local fork). Out of scope as the unit prompt says: chain tools (P2-U5), other data sources (P3-U2), the full discovery loop (P3-U4), the My Agents page (P1-U9), testnet and mainnet. BUILD_PLAN's row also names `get_goals_and_limits`; the unit prompt did not, so it waits for the goal form (P3-U1, recorded in D-213).
Summary: Agents now do real research. A scheduled Scan runs each funded agent in its sandbox, where it searches the web and reads pages through a metered data tools server, saves notes and ends with `complete_stage`; a separate narrator turns the run into an owner-readable activity entry whose numbers are checked against the records.
- Decisions (68fb355): D-212 accepts A-27 and A-28; D-213 tool servers and identity from the lease's gate token; D-214 Tavily and the read_url guard (resolves Q-24's provider); D-215 metering before the call, reversal of unanswered calls, 20 paid calls per lease; D-216 the scheduled Scan; D-217 the narrator. Assumptions A-29 (0.01 USDC per search, 0.002 USDC per page) and A-30 (narrator cost paid by the platform). W-7 gains the metering redesign (LiteLLM polled per agent every 2 seconds) and the scheduler's per-agent check.
- Config and schema (339ae65, ec3a5a7): `TAVILY_API_KEY` replaces the unused `SEARCH_PROVIDER` and `SEARCH_API_KEY`; `SCAN_INTERVAL_MINUTES` (360). Migration 0004: `platform.tool_calls` (the action log), `stage_records` (one per stage per lease), `thesis_notes`, `activity_entries`, and the `scan` task kind.
- Tool servers (7c587fd, efc4b35, 000be93): `packages/tool-server` hosts each MCP server statelessly on loopback, resolves the bearer token's hash to an active lease (401 otherwise, 503 if it cannot check), answers every error in FINAL_PLAN 4.4.1's shape (the SDK's own input and output validation errors included), and has the identity field lint. `services/data-tools`: `web_search` and `read_url` through Tavily, results marked `source: "web"`, `untrusted: true`, wrapped in markers the content cannot close, control and bidirectional characters stripped, capped. `read_url` refuses non-http(s), credentials, non-default ports, internal names and any host that is or resolves to a non-public address (44 guard cases). `services/platform-tools`: `complete_stage` and `write_thesis`, bound to the token's agent and lease.
- Orchestrator (03b7d9d, bce6dac, 810af70, e733e51, aa29711, 3fbbcfa): Hermes's config names the data and platform servers (sampling and elicitation off, parallel calls only on data), with URLs added at sandbox start; the gate routes `/mcp/data` and `/mcp/platform` with the gate token as bearer, dropping the sandbox's own authorization; the meter charges under the agent's lock with the call's row in one transaction and reverses failures (`usage_reversed`); the Scan task, the 30-second scheduler, a console trigger, and tool call and activity routes; the narrator on its own key (`aa-<namespace>-narrator`, kept by the startup sweep, L-84) with the number validator, one retry and a template fallback. 24-hour spend now includes tool charges.
- API (e0c523f): `GET /v1/agents/:id/activity`, public, text only (the facts behind each entry are never served).
- UI (168de2a, c158e3c, 7989013): ActivityFeed and ToolCallStatusBadge on /design first; the console gains "Run Scan", the Scan's result card, the latest entry as "Last action", and per-agent activity and tool call history. Every changed capture was looked at at both widths; the last action cell was clamped to three lines after the first capture showed a very tall row.

## 2026-10-06, P1-U9, My Agents (thin)
Status: done (local fork). This closes the credits step and Pass 1's Phase 1. Out of scope as the unit prompt says: trading and the PersonalAccount (Phase 2), pausing an agent, the spend chart, gas metering, testnet and mainnet.
Summary: Owners have a My Agents page at `/agents` that shows each agent they own, how to fund it, what it has spent and what it has been doing, with Run Scan and refund on the page. Fork starts now ride out the flaky upstream.
- Decisions (0df8378): D-218 My Agents and its owner-only summary; D-219 owner-requested Scans recorded in Postgres and queued by the orchestrator, one open Scan per agent; D-220 resilient fork starts. A-31: a Scan costs about 0.15 to 0.30 USDC, measured from P1-U7's live run (0.17 and 0.30).
- Forks (5d6a5e0, L-87): test forks and the playtest fork's runner check that the upstream serves the pinned block before each attempt, try up to six times with 1 to 16 second backoff, and alternate with `MONAD_RPC_URL_SECONDARY`, now allowed in every environment. A smoke run of a copy of the runner on port 8562, with a dead primary, came up on the secondary after one backoff with neither URL in its log; the playtest fork's own runner and files were not touched.
- Schema and orchestrator (38586fa, 52ad044): migration 0005 records who asked for each task and allows one queued or running Scan per agent with a partial unique index; the orchestrator queues Postgres-recorded Scans every two seconds.
- API (e898615): `GET /v1/agents/:id/summary` (owner session plus a fresh chain read): run status, credits, 24-hour spend, recent charges from the ledger (model, tool, and charges given back), the latest Scan, and the Scan minimum and estimate; `POST /v1/agents/:id/scan` refuses with `not_provisioned`, `credits_low` or `scan_open`.
- Design system (abdefec): RunStatusBadge, QrCode (drawn from @paulmillr/qr in the tokens; its test scans the rendered code back to the address), FundAgentPanel and SpendPanel, on /design first.
- Page (49d81c0): one card per owned agent from the index, with an owner session each: 2D art or the placeholder, tier, species, run status, the restricted and unrevealed notes, Fund your agent, Spend, Activity, a Configure link, and Run Scan and Refund, each behind a confirmation (the Scan's cost, the refund's ownership epoch), each with its result or error said on the card. Empty states: logged out, connecting, wrong network, not deployed, could not read, and no agents (with a link to /mint).
- Fix (42828ff, L-89): the reveal watch's 404s were what failed the live bee test at random in P1-U7 and again here.
- Tests: 14 page-rule tests, 6 component tests (including the QR scan), 5 control API tests (owner-only, refusals, staleness after a transfer), migration and scheduler tests. my-agents.spec.ts captures and scans every state at 1440px and 380px (logged out, no agents, unrevealed, funded, restricted, both dialogs) and covers Run Scan with its new entry, a refund and its result, a refused Scan, another wallet seeing nothing and making no owner request, and an agent that changes hands. `pnpm test:web:live:agents` passed on its first run in 1.4 minutes: mint, keeper reveal, paused while unfunded, the QR code matching the recorded funding address, 1 USDC credited with no other step, a Scan from the page with real Tavily and a model ending in an activity entry and charges, a refund paid to the owner on chain, no key in the page, and another wallet seeing no card while the owner-only routes answer it 401.

Every new capture was looked at at both widths. Heavy suites ran one at a time with dev:all stopped (by the supervisor's PID this time), after checking memory.
- Dev state: the playtest fork is as found (head 109670001, hash 0xb8fb...1a1b, nothing minted). The development database gets migration 0005 on the next start of the orchestrator. No test database, fork, tunnel, sandbox or namespace key is left. dev:all is stopped; anvil, Postgres, Redis and LiteLLM keep running.

## 2026-10-06, Phase 1 end playtest and tuning
Status: done (local fork). The owner finished the Phase 1 end playtest and was happy with the mint, the front end and the research activity; this session applied the tuning they asked for. Phase 1 is complete.
Summary: The local fork can reveal the Bee on demand, the Scan minimum covers a Scan's cost, the scrolling tab list is always focusable, and forks start reliably through a retrying proxy.
- Decisions (6d5c5f9): D-221 steered reveals on the local fork only; D-222 the 0.15 USDC Scan minimum; D-223 focusable scrolling tab lists; W-7 gains My Agents' per-agent sessions and polling.
- Steered reveals (b7a5ca4, f03a61a): on the fork the keeper plays Entropy, so its delivery searches for a number under which the chosen agent draws the chosen species, replaying AgentNFT's own draw. `LOCAL_FIRST_REVEAL_SPECIES` (default `bee`, or `random`) steers agent #1 on a fresh fork; the dev console's "Reveal next as" card steers the next reveal once (held in the orchestrator's memory, so a restart clears it). Guards: the variable exists only in the local environment and the loader refuses testnet or beta with a species set; `revealSteeringFor` returns none outside local; the chain client throws if given a steer without being built for the fork, and only the fork's client has a simulated delivery at all; the route and card exist only with dev actions. Tests: 8 unit tests (the draw replay, the search, the steering rules, none outside local, the chain client refusing), 3 config tests (default, refusal on testnet and beta, bad value), the API route (404 without steering or dev actions), console unit and e2e tests, and the keeper's fork test, where the real AgentNFT, fed the steered numbers, revealed agent #1 as the Bee and a later agent as the console's praying mantis. The orchestrator and My Agents live runs logged the keeper steering agent #1 to the Bee.
- Scan minimum (34d44e5): 0.15 USDC from one constant for the scheduler, console, owner and API.
- Tabs (f809788, L-90 resolved as L-93): the list scrolls inside a focusable, labelled wrapper that carries the frame; Radix keeps the list's own tabindex. Every tab capture is pixel-identical.
- Forks (562dc23, 2d14053, L-91, L-92): the upstream answered the pinned block with its transactions as null about half the time today, so D-220's whole-start retries were not enough; forks now reach it through a retrying JSON-RPC proxy, a child process for test forks (the deploy helper blocks its caller in spawnSync) and in-process in the playtest fork's runner. A smoke run of a copy of the runner on 8562 with a dead primary served through the secondary, with no URL in its log. The proxy's import-time path broke the console build once (L-92) and was fixed.
- Checks on the final tree: format, lint, typecheck; 1,226 vitest tests (none skipped); forge 69 of 69; test:fork passed; console e2e 46 of 46 (re-baselined: the agents page with the new card, the environment page listing the new variable; a confirming run matched); web e2e 116 of 116 (re-baselined: the restricted My Agents capture's 0.15 text; a confirming run matched); web live 7 of 7; My Agents live 1 of 1 (after L-94's test fix); the orchestrator live check 25 of 25. Heavy suites ran one at a time with no dev server, after checking memory.
- Dev state: the playtest fork is as this session found it: head 109670007 (0xd406...77d6), agent #1 minted by 0x683e...5f76 and revealed as the Earwig, with USDC funded, all from the owner's playtest (the mint was sent from the owner's wallet). This session sent no transaction to 8545; every test used a fork of its own. dev:all is stopped; anvil, Postgres, Redis and LiteLLM keep running. The running anvil was started before the proxy existed; the next start of the fork uses it.
Notes for the owner:
- Restart dev:all to load the new orchestrator, keeper and console code.
- On the current fork agent #1 is already revealed, so `LOCAL_FIRST_REVEAL_SPECIES` does nothing there; use the console's "Reveal next as: Bee" before the next mint, or start a fresh fork (steps in the handoff and the README).
Suggestions:

## 2026-10-06, Wallet reliability and OKX Wallet
Status: done (local fork and test builds; OKX against a real Privy login is for the owner to try).
Summary: The connect button no longer gets stuck, an account switch ends the old session instead of using it for the new address, OKX Wallet is a login option, and every wallet action uses the wallet the user chose.
- Diagnosis (reported before fixing): with a session for one address, an account switch in the wallet left the button connected on the new address, eligibility was asked for it with the old token (403 `wallet_not_linked` in the console) and /mint showed "Could not read" (the owner's bug); /agents owner sessions failed with `not_owner`; /configure showed the new address's agents. "Connecting" could spin with no action whenever Privy had a session but the wallet shared no account, and `login()` does nothing then. Only MetaMask was offered, actions went through wagmi's connector rather than an explicitly chosen wallet, and the button did not name the wallet. The mint progress was not reset on an account change. The mock hid all of it (any account with one token, no persistence, one wallet, nothing on `window.ethereum`).
- Design system (507e7db, 02d65ff): the wallet button names the wallet (in the chain chip's place from 1280 to 1536px, L-97) and offers Cancel while connecting; WalletNotice says what a connect waits for; /design specimens for both, and the declined or refused network add now ends with the manual steps (name, http://127.0.0.1:8545, 143143, MON).
- Wallet core (633fa13): one session core for Privy and the mock. It finds the login's wallet by its EIP-6963 rdns (Privy's provider for it as the fallback), reads the account and chain from that provider on its events and on focus, ends the session with a notice when the account differs from the login's or disappears, exposes the address and token only while they match, and sends every request, switch, network check and transaction through that provider. Privy's wallet list is MetaMask, OKX Wallet and detected wallets; wagmi, @privy-io/wagmi and react-query are removed (the lockfile drops only their entries, L-74). Mock tokens name their wallet; the control API's mock identity and the e2e fake API link only that wallet. Mint progress resets on an account change.
- Decision (docs commit): D-224 OKX support now, the session-per-address rule and the mock's two wallets; BUILD_PLAN moves OKX out of W-6 and adds OKX's network add and account switching to W-1's compatibility test, as does the pinned LESSONS section; the README covers OKX.
- Tests (6453bf3, 52cee48): wallets.spec.ts, 11 tests at both widths with MetaMask and OKX mocks installed together and OKX holding `window.ethereum`: both announce; login with each uses only that wallet (the other's request log stays empty); the login outlives a reload; minting goes through the chosen wallet only, for each; an account switch mid-session ends the session with a notice, the mint panel asks to connect instead of "Could not read", and a fresh login reads the new account with no 403; switching from MetaMask to OKX on another account; disconnect and reconnect; a locked wallet ends the session; a stalled connect shows the notice and Cancel; OKX refusing to add the fork shows the manual steps. Account-switch tests in the portal, My Agents and both live specs now expect a fresh login. New unit tests: the notice text, Cancel, the wallet name, WalletNotice, mock tokens. Login and shell screenshots re-baselined and looked at; shell and portal captures repaint first (L-99).
- Checks on the final tree: format, lint, typecheck; 1,233 vitest tests; forge 69 passed, 2 skipped (as before); test:fork passed; check-web-build passed (the real build has no mock marker or secret); console e2e 46 of 46; web e2e 146 of 146 (two consecutive runs after the last re-baseline); web live 7 of 7; My Agents live 1 of 1; the orchestrator live check 25 of 25 (report in evidence/p1-u7). Heavy suites ran one at a time with no dev server, after checking memory.
- Dev state: the playtest fork is as found (head 109670007, 0xd406...77d6, agent #1 the owner's Earwig); this session sent nothing to 8545. dev:all was not running and was not started.
Notes for the owner:
- Privy dashboard: nothing new is required. The wallet list is set in code (`walletList`), and external wallet login is already enabled (MetaMask logs in). If OKX does not appear in the login window, check Privy dashboard, your app, "Login methods", that "External wallets" is on, and if the dashboard lists individual wallets there, that OKX Wallet is ticked.
- Commit 507e7db alone does not build (L-98); 633fa13 right after it does.
Suggestions:
- Show the wallet's name on phones too, perhaps in the menu.
- Privy's `loginOrLink` could link a second wallet to the same Privy user instead of logging out; that needs a product decision on multi-wallet users.
Bugs: L-95, L-96, L-97, L-98, L-99.
