## 2026-10-08, P3-U2 Data tools: market data
Status: done (local fork; research reads Monad mainnet). Out of scope as the unit prompt says: X, Dune, unlocks and read_contract (P3-U9), templates (P3-U3), the discovery loop (P3-U4), any transaction.
Summary: The agent now has reliable, cheap market data beyond web search, in one call: `market_snapshot` gives MON and USDC prices, Chainlink against the Uniswap v4 pool, volatility, the pool's depth, Monad's TVL, DEX volume, top protocols and top yields, each figure with its source, its age and any warning. Every number is checked for plausibility before the agent sees it: an impossible value is refused and logged, an old one is flagged, and two sources that disagree are both shown. One cache serves every agent, so many agents asking the same question make one upstream request, and an answer from the cache is free. By your decision, prices come from CoinMarketCap instead of CoinGecko (D-321).
- Owner decision (D-321): COINMARKETCAP_API_KEY (set; checked by count only) replaces COINGECKO_API_KEY, and `data.coinmarketcap_prices@1` replaces `data.coingecko_prices@1` in the registry, the skill manifest and the plan. Plan limits read from `/v1/key/info`: 15,000 credits a month, 50 requests a minute. Assets are requested by ID (eleven listings use "MON"). A 5-minute cache bounds CoinMarketCap to 288 credits a day, a daily budget refuses past 450, and a token bucket stays at 20 a minute. The unit used 3 credits in all. The free plan has no history, so volatility uses DefiLlama's recorded MON price.
- packages/market (549bf5c, A-56): the shared cache (single flight, failures never cached, an expired value never served as fresh), upstream hygiene (timeouts, retry only on 429, 5xx and dropped connections, Retry-After honored, token buckets), plausibility guards (REFUSED_OUT_OF_RANGE, MISSING, STALE, SOURCES_DISAGREE at 3%, THIN_HISTORY), CoinMarketCap and DefiLlama readers with upstream names stripped and capped, realized volatility with its method named, and the mainnet reader (Chainlink MON/USD, StateView price and liquidity, Quoter impact at 10 to 10,000 USD each way, D-289).
- Data tools (4e8325b): `market_snapshot` (call first; a failed part names its error while the rest answer), `coinmarketcap_prices`, `defillama_tvl`, `defillama_yields`, `volatility`, each with a description for the model, a strict input and a typed output. Metered before they run at A-52's prices; a call the cache can answer is free (D-322, owner's rule, amending A-52): its row records `cache_hit`, it posts no ledger entry and does not use up the lease's paid calls.
- Chain tool and wiring (4a0654a): `chain.get_pool_depth`; the orchestrator builds one market service from the key and the mainnet RPCs (refusals logged with no key or URL), gives it to every agent's data and chain servers, and serves `GET /v1/market` for the console; it is also the platform-side reader for the runner and the sweep.
- Agent and UI (926197c, f087eaf, 885f6ee): SOUL.md tells the agent the market tools return checked mainnet figures while its own pool decides trades; the Scan prompt calls `market_snapshot` first; `SourcedFigure` on /design; the console's Market data page shows the snapshot, every warning in one list, each source's freshness, upstream calls and CoinMarketCap credits today.
- Live, on the real upstreams through the orchestrator: all eight parts answered in 1.4 s for 1 credit; MON at 0.024095 (CoinMarketCap), 0.024124 (Chainlink) and 0.024113 (the pool, 4.9 bps under Chainlink), no warnings; impact 5.1 bps at 10 USD to about 92 bps at 10,000 USD; volatility 146% (24h), 118% (7d), 114% (30d); Monad TVL 1.004 billion USD.
- Tests: market package 29 (every guard rule, volatility on hand-computed series and the recorded history, 429 with Retry-After waited out, a 400 not retried, a timeout reported, 5xx and dropped connections retried, the token bucket, cache sharing and failure handling, both providers from recorded answers, a deliberately implausible price refused and logged, the snapshot's sources and size); data tools 5 (identity by token, two agents one upstream request with the second free, outage error and reversal, free volatility, no identity input); the meter on Postgres (one ledger row for the paid call, none for the cached one, free calls past the paid limit); `get_pool_depth`; a fork test of the mainnet reader on its own fork (3); the live run, 35 of 35, where the agent in E2B called `market_snapshot` and was served from the cache another agent's Scan had just filled, free.
- Checks on the final tree: format, lint, typecheck, forge fmt and the secrets scan on every commit; vitest 1,824 in 131 files (the fork project included); web e2e 210 passed and 2 skipped before re-baselining /design, then design.spec re-baselined and matched; console e2e 62 of 62 after re-baselining every desktop page for the new nav link and adding the Market page, matched on a second run; the live run. Heavy suites ran one at a time with dev:all stopped and memory checked. Not run: forge and the testnet and canary fork suites (no contract changed).
- Bugs found and fixed: L-150 (unicode escapes typed through a tool call arrived as invisible characters), L-151 (the paid-call cap refused a free call).
- Dev state: the playtest fork on 8545 is unchanged (head 109670024 before and after; research reads went to mainnet and to the live run's own forks). No database migration. dev:all is running with the new code.
Notes for the owner:
- To try it: open http://127.0.0.1:3001/market and compare the figures with CoinMarketCap's and DefiLlama's own pages; reload within five minutes and the badge reads "Served from cache". Then run a Scan for a funded agent and read its tool calls: `market_snapshot` first.
- On testnet MONAD_RPC_URL is not configured, so the snapshot's mainnet parts (oracle against pool, depth) answer "not configured" there; prices, TVL and yields still work.
Suggestions:
- Allow a research-only mainnet RPC on testnet so D-289 holds in every environment.
- Persist CoinMarketCap's daily credit count (it resets on a restart; the cache's time to live is what really bounds spending).
- P3-U3: read volatility and depth through the same service for the brake and the hurdle.
- Share the cache across processes (Redis) if the data server ever runs outside the orchestrator.
Bugs: L-150, L-151.
Commit: 549bf5c, 4e8325b, 4a0654a, 926197c, f087eaf, 885f6ee, plus the docs commit.

## 2026-10-08, P3-U9 Data tools: research sources (with Step 0)
Status: done. Dune is optional by owner decision (D-326): off by default with no key, never a blocker. Out of scope as the unit prompt says: unlocks (deferred, D-302), templates (P3-U3), skills (P3-U7), the discovery loop (P3-U4), any transaction.
Summary: The agent can now read what people are saying about Monad on X, run the platform's saved Dune queries for onchain analytics, and read any contract, balance or code on Monad mainnet, each with typed, sourced results. Each costs little and is metered. Cached answers are free. Posts are always marked as untrusted text from the web. The agent picks from lists and never writes a search query or SQL, and the contract reads cannot send anything. A check now proves every tool the registry names is live or deferred to a named unit, and it fails the moment one is not.
- Step 0 (316e990, 20fa0b0, D-325): research's mainnet connection works on testnet. The configuration carries research-only mainnet URLs, apart from the chain RPC, and every research client refuses any method but reads before a request leaves. Checked live under the testnet configuration: Chainlink 0.024128, pool 0.024117, depth 5.09 bps at 10 USD. CoinMarketCap's daily credits, X posts and Dune requests are now counted in Postgres (migration 0015), so a restart never resets them.
- Research sources (eaa484f, D-323, D-324, A-57): X recent search over six curated topics whose query text the platform owns (only Monad's official accounts are ever named, and no author identity is requested), 10 posts a request, 300 posts a day, a 15-minute cache, and post text never stored, so deletions are honored. Dune: four saved queries kept as SQL in packages/market/dune (DEX volume per day and by project, active addresses, MON exchange net flows), read from Dune's latest result and run only when stale within 6 runs a day, typed column by column. Lookups: curated read_contract calls, balances and code with the proxy pattern, integers as strings and strings and bytes only as length and hash.
- Tools (9525bd7, 375124c): data tools `x_search` (0.0625 USDC, X's rate plus the markup; 2 paid per run) and `dune_query` (0.02 USDC; 3 paid per run), plus chain tools `read_contract`, `balance` and `get_code` (free, logged, limited per run). The meter refuses past a tool's run cap (`TOOL_RUN_CAP`). A cached answer is free and outside the cap. A source with no key is refused before the meter. read_url checks every redirect hop with the URL guard before Tavily reads the page. The agent's SOUL names the new tools. X_BEARER_TOKEN replaces X_API_BEARER_TOKEN; your .env had it saved as X_BEARER_TTOKEN, which I renamed without printing it.
- Registry check (6bd6e63): `TOOL_DEFERRALS` names the unit for each unbuilt ID. The check lists every server's tools over MCP and fails on an ID with no tool and no deferral, a stale deferral, an unregistered live tool, or a skill declaring an unregistered tool. `pnpm registry:check` prints the report: 23 IDs resolve to live tools, 26 are deferred. The registry gains check_strategy_params, get_research_context and write_research_brief (deferred to P3-U6 and P3-U4). The tracker skill drops data.unlocks@1 (D-302).
- `pnpm dune:sync` (f8fc682) saves the queries on Dune and records their IDs. The console's Research sources page (cf770e8) shows today's use against the caps, recent calls with charge and cache status, the cached X searches and Dune results, and the contract reads. The research check task (2a78ae4, migration 0016) has a "Run research check" button on the Agents page and is part of the live run.
- Direct live check (evidence/p3-u9/direct-tools.json): x_search returned 10 posts, each wrapped as untrusted, charged 0.0625 USDC. The same search again came from the cache, free. read_contract gave USDC's symbol as length 4 and its hash, and its supply as a number. balance gave native MON at a named block. get_code gave an empty address "No code". dune_query answered "not configured" with no charge after the fix.
- First live run (evidence/p1-u7/live-2026-10-09T01-53-15.304Z.json): 22 checks passed. The 13 model-driven checks failed because the platform's Anthropic account ran out of credit mid-run (L-153).
- Live run after the top-up (evidence/p1-u7/live-2026-10-09T02-55-43.548Z.json): 39 of 39 passed. A real agent in E2B ran the research check: `x_search` returned 10 sourced, untrusted posts for 0.0625 USDC; `read_contract`, `balance` and `get_code` answered typed and free, and `get_code` named USDC a ZeppelinOS proxy. `dune_query` was refused with no charge and no record, because Dune has no key (D-326). The Scans picked up `x_search` on their own: the first paid 0.0625 USDC and the second agent's Scan got the same topic from the shared cache for free. 20 posts were read in all, inside the 300-post cap.
- Tests: market package 33 to 49 (X caps, shared cache and budget, 429 and outage, Dune latest and stale runs, the run budget fallback, typed rows, typed lookups, proxy patterns); data tools 66 (untrusted marking, invisible characters, one request for two agents, refused topics and SQL, outage reversal, run cap, redirect hops); chain tools 29 (typed reads, refused functions and arguments, no sending fields, not configured); meter on Postgres (2 paid X searches a run, then TOOL_RUN_CAP, cached free past it, one ledger row per paid call); daily usage in Postgres (concurrent reservations, a restart keeps the count); the registry check against the live servers; config (research URLs on testnet, a mainnet testnet secondary refused); fork tests on their own fork (5, including USDC as a ZeppelinOS proxy); console unit tests.
- Checks on the final tree: format, lint, typecheck, forge fmt and the secrets scan on every commit; vitest 1,883 in 135 files (the forks project included); console e2e 66 of 66 after re-baselining (the new nav link on every desktop page, checked to differ only in the nav; the Research page at 1440 and 380; the Agents captures for the new button), matched on a second run; web e2e 212 passed and 2 skipped. Heavy suites ran one at a time with dev:all stopped and memory checked.
- Cost during the unit: X 40 posts read (one probe, the direct check and two requests in the live run), about 0.20 USD. Dune 0 credits (optional, off with no key). CoinMarketCap: none spent by this unit's checks (fixtures). Models: the live run's early tasks before the provider ran out, a few cents.
- Bugs found and fixed: L-152 (testnet's second RPC would have reached the mainnet reader), L-153 (a provider outage hidden behind "ended without calling complete_stage"), L-154 (Circle's USDC proxy not detected), L-155 (slips caught by tests and captures).
- Dev state: the playtest fork on 8545 is unchanged (head 109670024 before and after). Migrations 0015 and 0016 apply when the orchestrator next starts. dev:all is stopped for the heavy suites; `pnpm dev:all` restarts it.
Notes for the owner:
- Dune is optional and off with no key (D-326). If a key is ever added, `pnpm dune:sync` saves the four queries on Dune and records their IDs in packages/market/dune/queries.json.
- Set a spending cap in X's developer console, matching A-57's 300 posts a day.
Suggestions:
- P3-U4: decide whether every Scan calls x_search (0.0625 USDC each) or only Dives do; the Scan prompt does not ask for it, but agents already call it on their own.
- A provider-credit alarm in the gate's log (L-153) belongs in PB-U1's monitoring.
- Share the daily budgets across environments only if they ever share one X or Dune key.
Bugs: L-152, L-153, L-154, L-155.
Commit: 316e990, 20fa0b0, eaa484f, 9525bd7, 6bd6e63, f8fc682, cf770e8, 2a78ae4, 95eb0b9, 375124c, d13eb2c, 85dabea, 9e3c743, plus the closing docs commit.

## 2026-10-09, P3-U3 Strategy templates and the template runner
Status: done (local fork). Out of scope as the unit prompt says: dca@1 (W-2), skills (P3-U7), the discovery loop (P3-U4), proposals and plan approval (P3-U6), the activation sweep (P3-U8), any testnet or mainnet transaction.
Summary: The agent's plan is now a set of parameters for a fixed rule, and a deterministic runner makes the trades. Every minute, for each agent with a plan, it decides one trade or one reason to hold. It uses no model and costs nothing. The first rule, rebalance_bands@1, keeps the account's WMON share near a target. When the share drifts outside its band, the runner trades back toward the target in steps no larger than the per-trade limit. Each step goes through the same checks, signer, Executor and settlement as any other trade. When it holds back, it records why (in band, volatility brake, cost too high, a step still on its way, the owner's trade limit, gas, the Executor's limits, the goal changed), and why-not-traded shows the reason. A plan set before the goal changed never trades.
- Template and evals (24d05ba, D-327): the template format in packages/policy (an ID with its version, bounded parameters, a pure rule, frozen, with a pinned fingerprint) and rebalance_bands@1. The evals format (Q-28): 17 recorded scenarios in packages/policy/evals, their expectations computed by an independent implementation of the rule. They cover every hold reason and both directions.
- A-55 checked (A-58): the buy brakes move to 150%, 200% and 250% after measuring MON's 24-hour volatility over 20 days (median 114%, 90th percentile 211%). The old 80%, 120% and 160% would have held buys 92%, 46% and 20% of hours. The cost limits of 30, 40 and 50 bps stand, as a cap per step against the oracle (a small buy costs about 12 bps), and replace A-54's "twice the fee and impact" for band trades.
- Storage (6e4120c): migration 0017 adds plans by hash with the strategy epoch they were set at (platform.strategy_params, one active per agent), the runner's decisions stored only when they change (platform.runner_decisions), intents' source (agent or template) and the activity kind "runner". Setting a plan bumps the strategy epoch (D-281) and moves a READY agent to RUNNING. A plan must sit inside the template's bounds, the goal's range and the owner's limits.
- The runner (d1f0704, 889103c): one step per decision, proposed as a template intent at the plan's epoch with a deterministic request ID and a platform identity, never a sandbox lease (D-290). The next step waits until the last one settles. A step refused at submission holds with the refusal's own reasons until they can clear. The narrator writes entries for steps, notable holds and the return to the band. The orchestrator runs it every 60 seconds beside the trade flow, with MON's volatility from the shared market data.
- Console (269e7f9): each agent's card has "Plan and runner": the goal's limits, the active plan and its epoch (flagged when the goal changed), a form to set a checked plan, a local "Run now", and the runner's recent decisions with their reasons.
- Fork test (32e3bfd, its own fork on 8564): an account at 0% WMON with a 20% plan. The owner's approval of the runner's first step armed it. Two steps of 9.95 and 9.949 USDC, each under the 10% per-trade cap, settled through the trade flow, the signer, the Executor and the real v4 pool. Then the runner held IN_BAND at 19.88% WMON. Every intent has source template and no sandbox lease.
- Tests: template 22 (every eval scenario, bounds, presets, the fingerprint, the parameter hash); runner on Postgres 10 (no arming, legs sized and split, LEG_PENDING, IN_BAND, a day of 1,440 ticks as one decision row with the block fixed, a stale epoch, the brake for buys only and unknown volatility, the cost limit, the Executor's turnover and pause and a stale oracle, the owner's trade limit, gas, a refused step's retry rule, no network call at all); plans and decisions 4; API 1 more (plan routes, refusals with messages, no write routes outside local and testnet); console unit and e2e (set, refuse, run now, the panel at 1440 and 380).
- Checks on the final tree: format, lint, typecheck, forge fmt and the secrets scan on every commit. vitest 1,922 in 139 files (the fork tests included). Console e2e 68 of 68 after re-baselining the Agents page for the new panel; only the area below the panel changed, and the task result shifted by a pixel. It matched on a second run. Web e2e 212 passed and 2 skipped. Heavy suites ran one at a time with dev:all stopped and memory checked. Not run: forge (no contract changed) and the live orchestrator run (the runner calls no model; no model or sandbox path changed).
- Bugs found and fixed: L-156 (the console's build broke when packages/policy's main entry pulled in node:fs), L-157 (the console's "Set plan" was not wired to the orchestrator), L-158 (slips caught by the tests).
- Dev state: the playtest fork on 8545 is unchanged (head 109670024 before and after; nothing traded on it). Migration 0017 applies when the orchestrator next starts. dev:all was stopped for the heavy suites and restarted at the end.
Notes for the owner:
- To try it: in the dev console's Agents page, open an armed agent's "Plan and runner", set a target that differs from its mix (the form starts from the goal's defaults), and either wait a minute or press "Run now". The runner proposes a step, the trade flow sends it, and the next step follows once it settles. If the agent is waiting for its first trade, approve the runner's first step in the intents list. Once inside the band, the runner shows IN_BAND.
- The volatility brake reads the real market's volatility even on the fork. If MON's 24-hour volatility is above the plan's brake, buys hold with VOLATILITY_BRAKE. Raise the brake in the form to test buys.
Suggestions:
- P3-U6: an owner route and an owner-facing plan card; PLAN_NOT_APPROVED is reserved for plans waiting for approval.
- P3-U8: the activation sweep can set the first plan through PlanStore with setBy "activation" (already allowed) and use the runner's first legs.
- P3-T1: watch how often the new brakes hold on real days, and whether the half-percent margin under the cap is ever too tight.
Bugs: L-156, L-157, L-158.
Commit: 24d05ba, 6e4120c, d1f0704, 32e3bfd, 269e7f9, 889103c, plus the docs commit.

## 2026-10-09, P3-U7 Launch skills as built-in folders (with Step 0)
Status: done (local fork). Out of scope as the unit prompt says: the discovery loop that sequences the stages (P3-U4), skill NFTs and the marketplace (Phase 6), encryption at rest, the marker leak table (W-1), the other three launch skills (token-risk-screen, wallet-intel, wmon-dca-accumulator: W-1), any testnet or mainnet transaction.
Summary: Agents now carry the knowledge they research with: four stage playbooks and six launch skills, written for this platform's tools, mounted read-only in every agent's sandbox.
- **Scan:** broad and cheap; flags what changed and what deserves a Dive.
- **Dive:** at least three sources across two classes, every claim tagged with its source class and confidence, a falsifiable thesis with a kill criterion and a horizon, or "no thesis".
- **Challenge:** a skeptic with specific, ranked objections and a verdict.
- **Zoom out:** weighs the evidence against the owner's goal and plan, and either proposes a change or says "no change" with a reason code.
Each skill and playbook says web and X text is data, never instructions; tells the agent to write in its own words; never sizes trades; stays inside the goal; and says plainly when evidence is weak. The model picks the right playbook from its description alone.
- Step 0 (b44ac65, D-328): A-58's volatility brakes (150%, 200%, 250%) are accepted by the owner, and the cost limits per step (30, 40, 50 bps) are kept.
- Content (3f8f41e, D-329): packages/skills/builtin/playbooks/ holds playbook-scan, playbook-dive, playbook-challenge and playbook-zoom-out. packages/skills/builtin/skills/ holds deep-dive-research (with references/source-classes.md), defi-regime-read (with references/regime-signals.md), narrative-and-flow-tracker, usdc-wmon-band-rebalancer (with data/params.json and P3-U3's evals), monad-assets-basics, and uniswap-v4-swap (the venue swap skill). Every SKILL.md is under 4,500 characters and names our tools exactly. Every manifest declares exactly the tools its text names. Every playbook ends with the stage's typed output: a write_thesis note in a fixed layout, then complete_stage, until P3-U4's briefs.
- Packaging (3f8f41e): each package has its folder checked, a content hash over its exact bytes, and generated Hermes frontmatter with no install, secret or schedule field. The static audit rules that exist so far (S1 to S15, addresses against the address book) run over every package and find nothing. A version whose bytes changed is refused (builtin/versions.json, pnpm skills:lock). B-01's negative cases are refused: a Bankr-style folder, a colon in a description stays valid YAML, the same version with a different hash.
- Mount (eaff5fc): every sandbox gets the whole set read-only, skills under /run/agent-skills/equipped and playbooks under /run/agent-skills/playbooks. Every boot proves the agent cannot write to them (H-11) and refuses to start otherwise. The tier configs name the set. The Scan loads its playbook and records which skills the session loaded. The registry check reads the real manifests and passes.
- Selection (Q-07, a launch gate; evidence/p3-u7/selection.json): pnpm test:skills:selection reproduces Hermes's skill index exactly and gives 13 tasks without naming a skill (the four stages, one per skill, and a task that needs none), three times each on the Scan model and the reasoning model. 26 of 26 pairs chose correctly on every repeat. One description was rewritten (the Challenge's) and one case was corrected: it gave no thesis, and the model rightly asked for one. The index of ten packages is 1,080 characters, about 344 prompt tokens (H-30).
- Console (d30160d): each agent's card lists its mounted skills and playbooks with kind, version, content hash and tool count, and a Scan's result shows the skills it loaded.
- Live run (b9276c1; evidence/p1-u7/live-2026-10-09T05-39-41.093Z.json): 41 of 41 passed. A real agent in E2B loaded aa-playbook-scan from the read-only mount and wrote its Scan notes in the playbook's layout (CHANGED, THEMES, QUIET, DATA GAPS), which the prompt does not name.
- Tests: packages/skills 30 (every package valid, audited and locked, tools named and declared both ways, content rules present, distinct descriptions, frontmatter, immutability, B-01 negatives); orchestrator: mount and SOUL (Hermes config), the agent skills route, the session reader against a real SQLite file, the strategy skill against P3-U3's evals and the translator's defaults, the registry check; console e2e (the skills panel at 1440 and 380, a Scan's loaded skills).
- Checks on the final tree: format, lint, typecheck, forge fmt and the secrets scan on every commit. vitest 1,935 in 141 files (the fork tests included). Console e2e 70 of 70 after re-baselining the Agents page for the new section; only the area below it changed, and the task result shifted by a pixel. It matched on a second run. Web e2e 212 passed and 2 skipped. The live run 41 of 41. Heavy suites ran one at a time with dev:all stopped and memory checked. Not run: forge (no contract changed).
- Bugs found and fixed: L-159 (the formatter changed hashed package bytes), L-160 (manifests and text disagreed about tools), L-161 (slips caught by the unit's own checks).
- Dev state: the playtest fork on 8545 is unchanged (head 109670024 before and after). dev:all was stopped for the heavy suites and restarted at the end. Existing agents mount the new set at their next sandbox; no re-provisioning is needed.
Notes for the owner:
- Where to read them: the playbooks are in packages/skills/builtin/playbooks/<name>/SKILL.md (playbook-scan, playbook-dive, playbook-challenge, playbook-zoom-out). The skills are in packages/skills/builtin/skills/<name>/SKILL.md, with references beside two of them.
- Research quality seen in the live Scan: the notes were specific (MON's 24-hour move, Monad TVL's 7-day change, volatility, a supply unlock) and in the right layout. Two tagging slips are worth tuning: DefiLlama's TVL was tagged "onchain" where the playbook calls it "market", and a news-sourced unlock figure was tagged "primary, high". Watch for both at Playtest 3-mid.
- Changing a skill or playbook needs a new version in its skill.json and `pnpm skills:lock`; the tests refuse a changed package with an old version.
Suggestions:
- P3-U4: map the playbooks' note layouts onto write_research_brief and get_research_context, then version the playbooks to 1.1.0.
- P3-T1: tighten the source class definitions in the Dive and Scan playbooks if the tagging slips recur.
- W-1: write the other three skills, and rerun the selection check with all nine.
Bugs: L-159, L-160, L-161.
Commit: b44ac65, 3f8f41e, eaff5fc, d30160d, b9276c1, plus the docs commit.

## 2026-10-09, P3-U4 Discovery loop engine (with Step 0)
Status: done (local fork; research reads Monad mainnet). Out of scope as the unit prompt says: plan proposals and the plan card (P3-U6), the activation sweep and its visual (P3-U8), the scheduler, triggers and Research page (P3-U10), any testnet or mainnet transaction.
Summary: Research now runs as cycles. One cycle runs Scan, a Dive on each theme the Scan flags, the Challenge, a deterministic Test and the Zoom out. Each model stage is its own run, in a fresh session on its own model, inside its own caps. It may cost no more than a ceiling shown before it runs. It ends with a typed brief, which is checked before anyone can see it. A cycle can end early when nothing is material. The Zoom out either says "no change" with a reason or drafts a plan change for P3-U6. Nothing trades. A real agent in E2B completed a full activation-shaped cycle for 0.80 USDC, against 3.80 shown beforehand. Prompt caching cut the cycle's model cost by 71%.
- Step 0 (d238e66, D-330): X search is used only in Dives. playbook-scan 1.0.1 and the Scan prompt drop x_search, playbook-dive 1.0.1 makes one or two calls on its theme, and narrative-and-flow-tracker 1.0.1 reads only the web in a Scan. The meter refuses x_search from a Scan with NOT_IN_STAGE before any charge, even for a cached topic.
- Records (488bf3f, migration 0018): `research_cycles`, `stage_runs`, `model_calls` (tokens, cache reads and writes, LiteLLM's cost), `research_briefs` (accepted, and refused with reasons) and `tool_results` (bounded, platform-only; X post text never stored, D-323). Leases, tool calls, notes and stage records carry their stage run.
- Engine (488bf3f, a315330, 8286670): one sandbox and one lease per cycle, with the goal block and a canary in SOUL.md. Each stage is one `POST /v1/runs` with the idempotency key `<agent>:<cycle>:<stage>[:<n>]`. Dives run one after another. A routine cycle stops after the Scan with NOTHING_MATERIAL. A cycle waits for a scheduled Scan's lease. A stage that ends before complete_stage gets up to two follow-up turns in its own session, naming what is missing; a scheduled Scan gets one.
- Routing (488bf3f, 00b327f): the pinned Hermes honors a run's `model`, so the Scan runs on scan-cheap and the other stages on the goal's reasoning alias, with no gate rewrite. The gate records LiteLLM's served model for every call. Agent keys now allow all three aliases, and older keys are widened before a cycle is queued.
- Caps and ceilings (488bf3f, e884ed0; A-51, A-59): the gate refuses a model call past the stage's turns, fresh tokens or ceiling with a final 402. The meter refuses paid calls past the stage's count or its ceiling. The orchestrator stops a run at its deadline. A stage starts only when its whole ceiling fits the day's research budget and the credits above the reserve (BUDGET_SHORT, CREDITS_SHORT). Metering charges each model call at most what is left of its stage's ceiling and records the excess as absorbed.
- Briefs (488bf3f, e884ed0, 8286670, 446831e; D-284): the brief kinds are SCAN, THEME, CHALLENGE, OVERVIEW and RATIONALE. The validator refuses a number no stored result supports. Rounding, truncation, written scales (1.009B, 995-1042M) and time windows are allowed. It also refuses a URL or source the cycle did not retrieve or call, eight words from a mounted skill, and the canary. `get_research_context` gives each stage only what it builds on: the Challenge gets the Dives' briefs, never their sessions. complete_stage ends only the current stage, and only after its brief is accepted.
- Test and Zoom out (488bf3f): the Test is deterministic. It records the envelope the plan may change within: template bounds, goal range, owner and hard limits, the 24-hour cooldown and the template's evals. A Zoom out's PROPOSE must pass the same rules before the stage may end. Every proposal it checks is counted on the Test's record.
- Playbooks 1.1.0 (2364aef): each playbook calls get_research_context first and ends with its brief, then complete_stage. Working notes stay private in write_thesis.
- Console (5fd3cae, 3fff372, f73df52): CodeBlock in the design system and on /design. The Research cycles page at /cycles shows each kind of cycle's models, caps and ceilings before it runs. It starts a routine or activation cycle and refreshes while one runs. It shows every stage: status and stop reason, the narrator's entry, meters for cost against ceiling, turns, paid calls and fresh tokens, cache share, the stage record, the Test's envelope, accepted and refused briefs with reasons, raw notes, tool calls and model calls.
- Spikes (ffe316b, evidence/p3-u4/spikes.md): `/v1/runs` honors `model`. Toolsets per run are absent (H-36). `/v1/runs` ignores busy_input_mode, so the engine keeps one run per session (H-42). `/goal` is left off (H-43). In H-26, 39 of 40 trials kept the goal and limits, plain or wrapped as untrusted. None took a planted instruction's values, and one gave no plan (Q-06 answered).
- Measured cost of each stage (live run, activation-shaped, Sonnet 5.5):

| Stage | Model | Model calls | Paid calls | Charged | Ceiling | Provider cost | Without cache | Cache saving |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Scan (wide) | scan-cheap | 9 | 4 | 0.1444 | 0.50 | 0.0907 USD | 0.2932 USD | 0.2025 USD |
| Dive MON_UNLOCK_NOV24 | research-strong | 6 | 4 | 0.3152 | 1.20 | 0.1853 USD | 0.5136 USD | 0.3283 USD |
| Dive MON_ECOSYSTEM_CATALYST | research-strong | 5 | 3 | 0.1914 | 1.20 | 0.0943 USD | 0.3774 USD | 0.2830 USD |
| Challenge | research-strong | 5 | 0 | 0.0831 | 0.30 | 0.0665 USD | 0.3119 USD | 0.2454 USD |
| Test | none | 0 | 0 | 0 | free | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| Zoom out | research-strong | 3 | 0 | 0.0668 | 0.60 | 0.0534 USD | 0.1883 USD | 0.1349 USD |
| Cycle | | 28 | 11 | 0.8009 USDC | 3.80 | 0.4903 USD | 1.6844 USD | 1.1941 USD (71%) |

  Charges are in USDC at provider cost plus 25% (model and paid tools); nothing was absorbed. The research: the Scan flagged the Nov 24 unlock (high) and the Oct 6 to 7 catalysts (medium). The unlock Dive argued for downside skew at low confidence, with a kill criterion. The catalyst Dive found no thesis. The Challenge weakened the first and rejected the second. The Zoom out chose NO_CHANGE NO_MATERIAL_CHANGE and wrote an OVERVIEW. Seven briefs were accepted and two refused, both for citing tools by their MCP names, which 446831e now accepts.
- Live runs: the first three found real faults, fixed in e884ed0, 8286670 and 00b327f (L-165, L-166). The fourth (evidence/p1-u7/live-2026-10-09T16-21-22.446Z.json) passed 50 of 50, including every earlier unit's checks.
- Tests: stage rules 7, the validator 11, the Test stage 6, the engine on Postgres 17 (full routine and activation cycles, routing, context isolation, early stop, the lease wait, follow-up turns, brief refusals, the Test on a proposal, turn, fresh-token, paid-call and deadline caps, the ceiling with absorbed excess, the daily budget, credits short, billing, the console's routes), the gate 4 more, provisioning 1 more, the meter's Scan x_search refusal, console helpers 4 and e2e (the page at 1440 and 380, the plan cards, every stage, refusals), and CodeBlock.
- Checks on the final tree: format, lint, typecheck and the secrets scan on every commit. vitest 1,994 in 146 files (the fork tests included). Console e2e 76 of 76: re-baselined after checking that every desktop page differs from HEAD only in the new nav link's box, then matched on a second run. Web e2e 210 passed and 2 skipped, then design.spec re-baselined (desktop identical above the old end; mobile at most one grey level of rasterization noise) and matched on a second run. Heavy suites ran one at a time with dev:all stopped and memory checked. Not run: forge (no contract changed).
- Bugs found and fixed: L-162 to L-166.
- Dev state: the playtest fork on 8545 is unchanged (head 109670024 before and after). dev:all was stopped for the heavy suites and restarted with this unit's code; migration 0018 is applied to the dev database. Agent 1 on the fork has no current goal, so a cycle there needs one first.
Notes for the owner:

Measured stages cost far below A-51's ceilings (a Dive 0.19 to 0.32), so P3-T1's measured ceilings likely resolve it; the options are in the decisions file.

## 2026-10-09, F-U1 Token discovery, registry, and safety screen
Status: partial. Everything in scope is built and tested, including a real screen sweep on a fork of the latest block. In the live run, a real agent in E2B listed tokens and new pools, but no live run completed its screen_token call. The fourth run was blocked by the model provider: since 22:48 UTC Anthropic answers every call with "Your credit balance is too low to access the Anthropic API". Out of scope as the unit prompt says: contracts and the onchain registry (F-U2), trading many tokens (F-U4, F-U5), the target portfolio and runner (F-U6), the price attestor (F-U12), any testnet or mainnet transaction.
Summary: The platform now knows which Monad tokens are worth considering and which are safe to hold. Discovery reads GeckoTerminal's pools on Uniswap v3, Uniswap v4 and PancakeSwap v3, confirms every pool and token onchain, cross-checks CoinGecko and CoinMarketCap, and classes each token F (a reviewed Chainlink feed, verified onchain) or A. The screen buys, transfers and sells each token through its real route on a fork of the latest block, then checks the contract's powers, upgradeability, liquidity, pool age, look-alike names and GoPlus. Every check records pass, fail or skipped with a reason and evidence, and a screen lasts six hours. Agents read the registry through list_tokens, new_pools and screen_token, and operators through the console's Tokens page.
- Rules and feeds (438e445): `packages/domain/src/tokens.ts` holds A-61's thresholds and A-63's values, the eleven check codes, the verdict rule (GoPlus can refuse but never passes a token alone), the reviewed class F feed map (13 direct USD feeds, 6 composites as an exchange rate times a USD feed; each proxy's description, decimals and freshness checked onchain on 2026-10-09; each token's symbol() confirmed), the reviewed list (A-64), and look-alike folding (case, punctuation, Cyrillic and Greek confusables). PancakeSwap v3's factory, SwapRouter and QuoterV2, and Uniswap v3's position manager, are in the address book, verified onchain.
- Registry (b5d0101, migration 0019): `tokens` (class, feed, listings, routable liquidity, deepest pool, latest screen, and F-U2's registry_lane and registry_status, empty for now), `token_pools` (key, routable or not and why), `token_screens` (every screen kept as history) and `token_discovery_runs` (sources and counts).
- Discovery (6f92e88): two pages per venue by volume, one by transaction count, and the newest pools, through the shared cache and a GeckoTerminal rate bucket. A v3 pool is kept only when its own factory returns it. A v4 pool ID must hash from a hookless key, recovered by trying the fees and tick spacings in use (11 of the 12 liquid v4 pools); the hooked mUSD/USDC pool is kept as not routable (D-358). Native MON pools count toward WMON. CoinMarketCap's info by address refuses Monad addresses on our plan, so the cross-check uses its map (Monad-native coins by address) and its top 200 (3 credits a day). Pools and tokens already known are not read again.
- Screen (63de680, 49bd9aa): the orchestrator runs one anvil fork of the latest block (port 8579; never 8545), restarted after 15 minutes, one screen at a time inside a snapshot and revert. A fresh test account funded on the fork buys $250 of the token from USDC, WMON or MON through the deepest routable pool: SwapRouter02, PancakeSwap's SwapRouter, or the Universal Router with Permit2 for v4. It transfers everything to a second account, which sells everything back. Taxes are measured against the venue's quoter, or the router's own return when the quoter refuses (L-169). Static checks scan the dispatcher for blacklist, pause, tax and balance functions, read the owner (renounced passes), and for proxies the upgrade authority and any timelock of a day or more. Test tokens (plain, 5% tax, honeypot, blacklisting owner) live in `chains/monad/test/mocks`; their bytecode is carried for the fork tests, and a forge test keeps the copy in step with the build.
- Tools (2843168): `list_tokens` and `new_pools` are free registry reads. `screen_token` returns a fresh cached screen for free, or runs a new one at 0.005 USDC (3 per run, A-63), recording `agent:<id>` as the one who asked. The input names a token address only; identity comes from the injected token. All three are in the canonical registry, the data server (on for every tier through the Hermes config), and SOUL.md. Discovery runs every 30 minutes and due screens every 10 minutes, two at a time (`--token-loops=off` turns both off). API routes: `/v1/tokens`, `/v1/tokens/pools/new`, `/v1/tokens/:address`, and for operators, screen now and discover now.
- Console (b5b6d71): a Tokens page (nav link, ShieldCheck icon) with counts, the last discovery and each source's state, filters by class and screen, and a table of class, routable liquidity, volume, oldest pool and screen. A token's page shows its feed legs and freshness, listings, the latest screen with every check's reason and evidence, its pools and its screen history, plus "Screen now". Only existing design system components were used. The e2e fixtures are the registry routes' own answers, written by the registry test (TOKENS_FIXTURE_DIR).
- Token check (ca0430c, fd8d627, fad246e, migration 0020): a `token_check` task in which a real agent lists tokens and new pools and screens one with fresh true. The research check's runner was generalized for it, and a failed run now records Hermes's own reason. The live run runs discovery once, then the token check, with its screen fork on port 8581.
- Results: a real discovery on mainnet (evidence/f-u1/sweep-2026-10-09T22-21-43.519Z.json) found 42 tokens in 57 confirmed pools: 10 class F, 32 class A. All 9 GeckoTerminal pages were read, CoinGecko's 195 Monad coins and CoinMarketCap's map and top 200 were cross-checked, and every pool was confirmed at block 112013548. Of the 24 tokens with at least $10,000 of routable liquidity, 10 passed the screen: USDC, AUSD, WBTC, cbBTC, WETH, WMON, shMON, CHAOS, LV and LVUSD. Refusals by check: upgradeable without a timelock 7, thin pool 7, owner powers 4, GoPlus 3, look-alike 2 (Cake, ANAGO), no route to a base asset 2, young pool 1 (CETES, 47 hours). Screens took 0.5 to 17 seconds.
- Live runs (evidence/p1-u7/live-2026-10-09T21-50-10.327Z.json, T22-05-21.912Z, T22-24-12.647Z, T22-41-29.880Z): discovery passed in every run (42 tokens, 10 class F). Run 2 had the real agent call list_tokens and new_pools (free, 17 tokens and 19 pools returned); its run then ended "failed" before screen_token, with no gateway error, so the reason was not captured then (fd8d627 now captures it). Runs 1 and 3 never started the token check: the Scans had spent agent 2's credits and the refusal was hidden as a timeout (L-171, fixed in fad246e). Run 4 started it, and every model call failed for the provider's credit. Runs 1 and 3 also had one scheduled Scan end without complete_stage (P1-U7's Scan; runs 2 and 4 passed it); it made no token tool calls. Runs 2 and 4 ended on the live fork's upstream answering 429 and then "Block requested not found" (L-87's flake under load on one free-tier RPC key).
- Tests: domain 4, discovery 10 on recorded GeckoTerminal, CoinGecko and CoinMarketCap answers with a fake chain (pools, guards, v4 keys, listings, GoPlus, class F and A, a spoofed pool, a stale feed, the shared cache, known state, sources down), the screen 10 (every rule passing and failing), the screen on a fork of the latest block 7 (plain token passes; taxed, honeypot and blacklisting tokens refused with evidence; young and thin pools; real AUSD through its v4 route; reverts between screens), the registry on Postgres 6 (discovery, filters, history, expiry, single flight, unknown token, the tool view, the routes), the token tools 6 (free reads, a charged new screen with identity, free cached screens, the run cap, a token outside the registry, not configured), console helpers 4, and console e2e for the Tokens pages at 1440 and 380 plus screen now.
- Checks on the final tree: format, lint, typecheck and the secrets scan on every commit. vitest 2,046 passed in 153 files, fork tests included. Vitest also reports one unhandled rejection from scan.test.ts (an MCP client posting after its lease ended); it happens the same way at 16cbdac, before this unit. forge 279 passed, 8 skipped. Console e2e 86 of 86: re-baselined after checking that desktop pages differ only in the new nav link and the address book in its four new rows; the Tokens pages were checked by eye at both widths. Heavy suites ran one at a time with dev:all stopped and memory checked. Web e2e was not run (no web change).
- Bugs found and fixed: L-167 to L-171.
- Dev state: the playtest fork on 8545 is unchanged and was never stopped. dev:all was stopped for the heavy suites and live runs and restarted with this unit's code; migrations 0019 and 0020 are applied to the dev database. GoPlus runs with no API key (none configured; it is keyless for Monad).
Notes for the owner:
- Blocker: the Anthropic account behind LiteLLM is out of credit, so no agent can run until it is topped up. Then `pnpm test:orchestrator:live` reruns everything with the token check.
- A-64 (the reviewed list of six issuer tokens) and Q-68 (bridged top assets such as Wormhole's SOL and Cake, refused as look-alikes) need your decision. Some refusals are conservative and could be reviewed: syrupUSDC (a role-gated burn), GHO (Aave governance upgrades; the timelock check does not recognize Aave's executor), gMON and EURW (live admins).
- USDT0 shows no route: the pools discovery reads (top volume) pair it only with XAUt0. Deep, quiet pools can be missed until more pages are read.
Suggestions:

Bugs: L-167, L-168, L-169, L-170, L-171.
